- **TWO WRONG VERSIONS OF ONE COLLECTION DAY, AND SIXTY NOTICES NOBODY HAS READ.** At a stop at the bottom of the Cleeve road on Sunday the eleventh of June the man in a fleece told a stranger that the Thursday was moving, that this parade was going onto a different day from the middle of the month, and that it was the road works. **The man who puts shutters up told a woman outside a closed shop that it is the Tuesday, and that it is not one parade but two hundred yard of the top of the road, because if one street moves and the next does not you have a street where nothing comes.** One claims a parade and one claims a stretch of road, the two are about eight hundred yard apart, and only one of the two men was in the road. **The notice that would settle it went on about sixty doors from the Thursday off a lorry carrying no lettering at all, four men, knocking and putting it on anyway, about three quarters of an hour, and nobody in the shop and nobody at the stop saw a man get out of that lorry.** The shutterman put his in his pocket without reading it and binned it the same night; the man who works in that shop tore his off because it was under the foot of his ladder and binned it with the paper. **Nobody in that parade has read one, and the woman who works in a shop, who has heard both versions, says that is not noise and is not two things either: it is the weather, and the only thing that could tell them which is right is on sixty doors nobody has read.**
- **A FIFTH THING A BUILDING DID, AND A BIN PUT BACK ON TWO STUDS.** A glazed lantern stands about four foot back on the flat roof over that first-floor corridor, and nothing is sealing round the exposed outside edge of its curb. **In the week of the twelfth of June water came down the wall of the stairwell, along the underside of the ceiling, over the head of two doors, and off a wall in a crescent the height of a chair back, and took the paper off the skirting in one strip about two foot long.** The woman of about fifty-five found it on the Tuesday of that week, on her own landing, with a washing-up bowl of her own under it, and she has emptied it several times a day every day since, and has a second bowl for the jugs and a towel round the first one, and there is a wet mark on the ceiling the size of a tea tray that has not got bigger at all since the bowl went under. Nobody has been on that roof and nobody has had a ladder out of the back of that building in June. **The woman in the room next door along came out on the Wednesday, saw the bowl, said there was a smell off that wall in the morning, and shut her door.** Nothing was offered and nothing was refused. **Three streets off, on the Friday morning, the man in his forties found a wheelie bin on its side in the middle of a passage with a puddle under it, hooked it back over two studs, went back for a key, took a quarter of an inch of play out of the lower stud, pulled on it, and the whole thing came off the wall in one piece. He did not listen at it, did not look inside it and knocked on neither of the two front doors.** The two repairs are four hundred yard apart and no living person connects them.
- **THREE GATES IN CLEAR WRAPPERS IN THREE YARDS ON ONE LANE, AND A NUMBER SAID OUT LOUD.** A painted timber gate, four foot by three, white, shipped in a polythene sleeve with brown tape at its waist and its clear wrapper, and **in the wrapper there is nothing that could tell you how long it has been on a shelf or whose it is.** One was left in a yard near the top of that lane in the third week of May. One was left higher up that same lane in the first week of June, and the man in his thirties told the woman who works in a shop about it in four sentences from the alley and she did not go and look. One was left in the yard by the car park on Monday the nineteenth of June. **He counted them on the Wednesday morning by walking the lane, and the number is three, and it is the first time he has ever said a number like that out loud to anybody.** The first and the third are gone: a green mark on a gable wall, a wet rectangle and a scuff of white and a scrap of wrapper on a kerbstone. The second is still there, gone a dirty grey. **THE ONLY PERSON WHO WATCHED ONE OF THEM GO IS THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP, FROM HER OWN WINDOW, AT ABOUT SIX IN THE MORNING ON THE TUESDAY, AND SHE TOLD NOBODY AND DOES NOT KNOW THE MAN WITH THE BARROW'S NAME, AND HE FOUND OUT ONLY AFTER HE HAD COUNTED THREE, AND HE DID NOT ASK HER TO SAY SHE HAD TOLD HIM.** He got the pad out in the van and shut it again, and gave the reason: a pad carrying a number has no home in this borough.
- **A PROMISE AT A DOOR ABOUT A FENCE, AND AN ADMISSION.** Three lengths of close-board are lying against the brick of a yard at the low end of Cleeve Street, out of a job, not put up, the fixings still in them at one end. the opening measures about four foot across shoulder height and about nine people went through it on that Sunday morning. **On the Friday before, at a door, to a man in a fleece, who was up on the step, the man in his thirties said he would close that opening before the month is out.** Nine days later he stands in the road and works out in about four seconds what was wrong with the sentence, and it is that the mistake was made off the doorstep rather than in the yard, and a promise made about another person's ground is a promise about their ground. **A woman with a clipboard has that gate written on her sheet twice with two different numbers against it and knows it is one gate, and a man in a fleece told her a man had said he would have it closed before the end of the month. She does not say he did it. He puts it the way he said it and will not be let off it, because the man in the fleece has already said to her what he said, and there is no arrangement of the facts that leaves him anything but the one that was misheard.** She asks whether he will do it and he says no, rules out the two easy reasons first, gives the real one, and then says the thing that puts it out of reach of being a conversation: no third party exists who could have heard it, him and a man in a fleece, and that man is not here. **She wrote about a quarter of an inch on the edge of her sheet, looked at it, rubbed it out with a thumb, and said nothing about having done it.** He took the pad out of its box in the road and shut it again. **On the second of July the three lengths are still against that wall and the gap is still four foot wide.**
- **A CARPET TAKEN IN AND USED ONCE, AND A CHEST HELD OUT AND REFUSED.** A wool carpet about nine foot by six, dark, its backing still fixed to its face in staples, came out of a front room on the Cleeve road in May and went up that outside stair in the second week of June with the woman of about fifty-five on the lead end, because it is a single and a half and it goes round the corner at the fire door. **She had said, before it came up, that no carpet was going in that room, and no carpet went in that room; what lay on that floor for three weeks was somebody else's carpet, and she laid it and took it up herself.** She laid it herself on the Sunday, got a corner up off the skirting with her fingernails on the twenty-third because she wanted to sweep under it, it came away in a shape and would not go back, and on the thirtieth she took it in hand once more and stood it edgeways where the wall takes the light. **on the very same morning, the same man brought a chest up that stair — about two foot by a foot by a foot, light wood, never painted, a lid and a catch — put it down on the step outside her door, knocked twice, and said the whole of what he had come to say and then said she could say no. She said no. He said right you are and went down with it and shut the street door and put it under a folding stool and two trestles in the back of his van.** Four seconds passed between her no and him turning round and nothing was said in them by either person, and neither has said a word about it since. **He did not ask, and he gave the reason to the woman who works in a shop at a shop door: a refusal given at once is a refusal, and a refusal given after thought invites a question is about a second, and a person who stood there a minute would want to know why, and you do not obtain the reason.** **AND THE MACHINE IS NOT IN THAT CHAPTER AT ALL: 0332 IS A CARPET, A CHEST AND A REFUSAL, THE CARD FOR IT SAID THE SECOND THING MAY NOT BE A VACUUM CLEANER, AND NOTHING IN 0332 OR ANYWHERE ELSE ON THE PAGE PUTS IT DOWN THAT STAIR. IT IS STILL LYING ON ITS SIDE IN THAT CORNER WHERE THE LANDING WIDENS BY THE FIRE DOOR, WHICH IS WHAT 0322, 0324 AND 0329 SAY, IT HAS NOT BEEN RUN AGAIN AND THE FILTER HAS NOT BEEN EMPTIED, AND THE MAN WHO CARRIED IT UP NEVER FOUND OUT THAT IT HAD BEEN RUN ONCE.**
- **ONE DAY, FOUR ROOMS WITH SOMEBODY IN THEM, AND SOMETHING SAID ALOUD.** On Monday the third of July the lad of about eighteen was in five places on one day and not one person put a single task to him in any of them, and four of the five rooms had somebody in them. In the kitchen at the back of the parade **the man who cooks in it was at the far end of the bench, cup in hand, the radio on quietly on low, and three items were waiting in that sink rather than the usual two, and nothing was passed to him and no destination was given to him and he knows, and at about ten past eight the man put his own cup in that sink and went to work with the cloth still in his hand.** In the greengrocer's **a man brought a sack of leeks in at twenty to twelve, set it on the counter, announced to nobody in particular that the goods were for the counter and on the account, and went out, and the sack stood where he put it for two hours and nobody asked anybody to shift it**, and the woman came off the telephone and carried it into the cold room herself. In the house at the end of Marsh Lane the two of them said good morning and that was the whole of it. In the corridor by the water **there has been nothing on that floor to mop since before Christmas and he went the length of his mop regardless**, and the woman of about fifty-five went down with two empty jugs at about a quarter to ten and came back at ten past ten with both full and a kettle over her other arm, **and he did not speak to her this time, because he put a remark to her back in May, got no reply, and the whole business stayed with him for a fortnight afterwards.**
- **AND WHAT HE SAID, AT TEN TO SEVEN, IN THE HALL OFF THAT PARADE, ACROSS THE TABLE, NOT QUIETLY, TO A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY-TWO WHO HOLDS THAT KEY, WITH THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES AND THE MAN WHO WORKS NIGHTS BOTH ABLE TO HEAR: THAT the neighbour in that same waterfront block HAS NOT BEEN SEEN IN THAT CORRIDOR SINCE A WEDNESDAY NINETEEN DAYS AGO, THAT SHE GOES PAST THAT BOWL TWICE A DAY, THAT she is the only other occupant of that landing, and THAT for seven months the pair have exchanged nothing whatever, and HE HAS BEEN THERE THE WHOLE TIME AND CANNOT TELL WHETHER THEY ARE NOT SPEAKING OR NEVER WERE. NOBODY ANSWERED HIM. The woman with the key said it was an unusual quantity of attention to have paid to two neighbours, and The man in his forties agreed, and that closed it.** He went at a quarter to seven and covered the two miles home empty-handed, and his bucket and mop were still in that store when he came back for them at half past four, and no resident of that building ever enters that store.
- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A card has been in a slot eight hundred and sixty-nine days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post eight hundred and seventy-one days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 325–334 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The counter in the vestibule is in no scene in this batch, answered nothing and was not entered, and **the drawer under it is not opened, not counted, not asked about and not mentioned, and the word *drawer* is at zero in all ten chapters, as is the word *envelope*.** The rota is not rebuilt and the 11:00–13:30 row is not mentioned. The second line under Bram Osei's Thursday has not been looked at. The board is at seven of nine. The ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and untold. The buff file of 14 October 2011 is not opened and four people have seen the bay number and the date on that cover. Paragraph 4.11 is not answered and the compliance week is not verified. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines removed, the stem *drain* is at zero in all ten, the word *spring* is at zero in all ten, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven.** The order is not in force and was not brought back to life.

- **AND THE SIX OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE WAS ANSWERED. From 0184, which dated thing will be the first somebody can prove — **still open, and it went the other way again: a thing that cannot be checked got one room further on the third of June, the man in his forties would not let it be counted, and the man who works nights asked one question and was not answered.** No fifth of anything has been produced, the third answer was not corrected, the fourth was not called a fifth, and the twenty-eighth of June was not mentioned. From 0174, which of the eight will be the first somebody decides to keep — untouched. From 0164, whether the page or the chair is the first thing a person is asked to agree to — **a pad came out of a van and was shut again, and a woman wrote a quarter of an inch on the edge of a sheet and rubbed it out with a thumb and said nothing about having done it, and nobody was asked and nobody asked anybody.** From 0192, which of two opposite shapes this city has to stop, and what a person has to be holding to stop each — **the second of them is still nothing.** The volume's own, about a permission, an obligation and a right of return — untouched, and the word *permission* is at zero in all ten chapters. And the sixth, about a document and not a person — **no piece of paper was created in this batch; the things that came were a white van, a bar off a frame, a length of sash, a washing-up bowl, a bin on two studs, a sack of leeks, a chest with a catch on it, a pad that was not written in and a bit of polythene on a kerb, and nobody has a copy of any of it.**

- **AND FOUR FLAGS GO TO A HUMAN AND NONE OF THEM IS A WRITER'S. THE NAME OF THE CITY, WHICH THE PROSE CALLS TIDEGLASS, AND *NACRE* AND *RIVER STACKS* APPEAR NOWHERE IN CHAPTERS 325–334. WHAT VOLUME 05 IS CENTRALLY, WITH THE OPERATIVE RULES BINDING AND NO ROOT TERM EXPIRED AND NO RECLAMATION SEASON OPENED AND THE WORD *RECLAMATION* IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH. THE BOUNDARY, WHICH IS A HUMAN'S: `outline/series.md` GIVES VOLUME 04 AS 145–192, VOLUME 05 AS 193–240, VOLUME 06 AS 241–288 AND VOLUME 07 AS 289–336, THE PAGE HAS 195 THROUGH 334 IN THE VOLUME 05 DIRECTORY, THE TWO DISAGREE AT BOTH ENDS, THERE IS NO FIGURE FOR THE END OF VOLUME 05 IN EITHER, THE FIGURE 242 IS USED BY NOBODY, NO CHAPTER IS MOVED BETWEEN DIRECTORIES, NO OUTLINE IS REWRITTEN, NO VOLUME 06 OUTLINE AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT EXISTS, AND CARD 0334 IS NOT A CLOSE. AND THE SPELLING OF THE THIRD OF THE EIGHT PLACES, WHICH WAS NOT TOUCHED IN THIS BATCH BECAUSE THE EIGHT PLACES ARE NOT READ OUT IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, SO NEITHER SPELLING APPEARS AND THE FLAG CARRIES FORWARD UNDECIDED; **THE CENSUS OF THE STATE FILES THAT PRINT SPELLINGS IS CARRIED AT §11 OF THE `state/open-threads.md` BLOCK AND IS NOT REPEATED HERE AND NO SPELLING IS NAMED IN ANY FILE.****

- **AND TWO FLAGS WERE TAKEN IN CONTINUATION 0014 AND BOTH RULES WERE KEPT. **CARD 0334 ASKED FOR *A BOROUGH, IN A MONTH*, AND ITS OWN COUNT GAVE IT FORTY-ONE DAYS FROM ITS FIRST DATE, WHICH IS A MONTH AND A FORTNIGHT, AND THE BINDING RULE WAS APPLIED ANYWAY: THE WORD *SPRING* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS, NO CHAPTER CALLS ITS OWN MONTH A TURN OR A CHANGE OF FORTNIGHT OR A CHANGE OF ANYTHING, AND 0334 WAS GIVEN ORDINARY WEATHER INSTEAD, WHICH IS THE HOTTEST EVENING OF THAT YEAR SO FAR AND A SHOWER THAT DID NOT REACH THAT STREET. **AND THE PROMPT'S OWN BOUNDARY PAIR WAS ADDED UP RATHER THAN COPIED AND CAME OUT CORRECT, WHICH IS NO REASON TO TRUST THE NEXT ONE: THE 0013 PROMPT PRINTED ITS OWN ARITHMETIC ONE DAY LATE AT BOTH ENDS. COUNT THE GAPS AGAIN. **THE NEXT PHASE IS A CONTINUATION BATCH, CHAPTERS 335 ONWARD, DATED FROM SUNDAY THE NINTH OF JULY 2017, WHICH IS 0334's OWN DATE PLUS ONE, AND IT IS NOT A VOLUME CLOSE. **AND TWO FINDINGS OF THE MEASURING PASS ARE FLAGS AND NOT CLOSURES: THE LAD IN FIVE ROOMS HAS NOW BEEN RUN SIX TIMES AND THE STANDING INSTRUCTION SAYS THE DEVICE ITSELF MUST CHANGE RATHER THAN THE FURNITURE, AND THE CARD FOR 0333 ASKED FOR THE FURNITURE; AND A MAN WHO SAYS HE WILL DO A THING HAS NOW BEEN RUN IN THREE CHAPTERS, 0311, 0321 AND 0331, AND A WRITER MUST READ ALL THREE IN THAT ORDER BEFORE WRITING A FOURTH. **THE MEASURING SCRIPT FOR THIS BATCH FAILED ITS OWN POSITIVE CONTROL TWICE BEFORE IT PRODUCED A USABLE FIGURE, BECAUSE THE TOKENISER WAS WRITTEN AS `[A-Z0-9']+` AND APPLIED TO LOWERCASED TEXT, WHICH MATCHES ONLY DIGITS AND APOSTROPHES AND MADE EVERY LINE OF DIALOGUE RETURN A RUN OF NINE QUOTATION MARKS. THE METHOD IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN AT §12A OF THE CONTINUATION 0014 BLOCK, AND A CONTROL THAT COMES BACK NIL IS A WRONG SCRIPT AND NOT AN EMPTY REPOSITORY. **AND ALL SIX OF THOSE FIGURES ARE WITHDRAWN AND ARE NOT THE FIGURE. A REVIEW REPAIR ON 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 RE-TOOK ALL SIX BY THE METHOD WRITTEN DOWN AT §12A AND COULD NOT REPRODUCE ONE OF THEM, AND THE SIX THAT REPLACE THEM ARE AT §13B OF THE CONTINUATION 0014 BLOCK: 0 RAW AND 0 REUSES, 38 RAW AND 0 REUSES, 42 / 29 / 18 RAW AND 0 REUSES IN THE THREE NAMED WINDOWS, 3 RAW AND 0 REUSES AGAINST THE SIX STATE FILES, 3 RAW AND 0 REUSES AGAINST EVERY NON-CHAPTER FILE, AND SIX IS THE COUNT OF RUNS AND NOT A RUN. **THE WITHDRAWN SET WAS WRONG IN A WAY THAT HID A CHAPTER: THE 315–324 WINDOW WAS PRINTED AT NIL REUSES WITH A NIL LONGEST RUN AND CAME BACK ON RE-TAKING AT FORTY-TWO RAW SPANS WITH A LONGEST RUN OF SEVENTY-EIGHT TOKENS, AND 0334 WAS TRANSCRIBING 0324 VERBATIM IN FOUR SPANS OF 78, 67, 20 AND 14 TOKENS. THE NAMED 0013 HAND-OVER REUSE IS NOT AMONG THE THREE THAT SURVIVE, SO A WRITER WHO FINDS IT SHOULD CHECK THE SCRIPT BEFORE REPAIRING ANYTHING.******

- **AND A REVIEW OF CHAPTERS 325–334 ON 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND FOURTEEN THINGS AND REPAIRED THE EIGHT THAT ARE OURS, AT SOURCE, WITHOUT RESTARTING THE BATCH AND WITHOUT CHANGING ONE CARD, DATE, DAY-COUNT, STANDING, REFUSAL OR FLAG. THE FULL LIST IS §12F AND THE FIGURES ARE AT §13C OF THE CONTINUATION 0014 BLOCK, WHICH SUPERSEDES §13B. WHAT A LATER WRITER NEEDS FROM IT IS THIS: FOUR STATE FILES WERE HOLDING FOUR CHAPTERS' PROSE IN THE CHAPTERS' OWN WORDS AND ARE NOW DESCRIPTION; 0330 NOW MARKS ON THE PAGE THE REVERSAL OF 0327'S REFUSAL TO COUNT, WHICH WAS THE POINT OF 0330 AND WAS SILENT; 0333'S ARITHMETIC AND 0331'S FIGURE ARE RIGHT; 0327's CLOSING NO LONGER PARAPHRASES ITS OWN OPENING; 0334 SPENDS A GUTTER INSTEAD OF THE TWO SLATES 0013 ALREADY SPENT; AND THE MOTIF *A FRIDAY* IS AT ZERO IN THE TWO-WORD FORM ONLY, WHICH IS HOW THREE FILES PRINTED IT, AND 0331 CARRIES ONE AND 0332 NONE. **SIX OF THE FOURTEEN ARE DEFECTS IN THE CARDS THAT ASKED FOR THEM AND NONE IS A FINDING AGAINST A CHAPTER** — 0329 RE-PLACES THE SPENT VACUUM CLEANER, 0326 RE-RUNS THE SPENT TROLLEYMAN-AND-FRIDGE UNDER A NEW NAME, 0332'S CARD ASKS FOR THE SPENT SHAPE WITH A CARPET, 0330'S WRAPPED GATE IS 0013'S FOLDED FRAME IN DIFFERENT MATERIAL, 0331 BREAKS TWO OF ITS OWN CARD'S INSTRUCTIONS, AND 0334 SPENT TWO SLATES — **AND A CARD THAT ASKS FOR A SCENE ALREADY WRITTEN MUST SAY WHAT IS NEW ABOUT IT, AND SIX CARDS IN TWO BATCHES DID NOT. THE SEVENTH IS `state/phase-ledger.json`, WHICH IS FIVE VOLUMES STALE AND IS OWNED BY THE WORKFLOW AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.****

**AND THE §13B FIGURES ARE NOW THEMSELVES SUPERSEDED IN PART, BECAUSE A CHECKPOINT PASS ON THE SAME DAY BUILT THE EXCLUSION DETECTOR §13B ITEM 8 ADMITS WAS NEVER WRITTEN, AND A DETECTOR FINDS WHAT A HAND TALLY MISSES. THREE REUSES OF PROSE THAT §13B REPORTS AT ZERO REUSES EXISTED IN 0329 AND 0334 AND HAVE BEEN REPAIRED AT SOURCE IN THE CHAPTERS, AND A FOURTH DEFECT, AN HOUR IN 0326'S CLOSING BLOCK THAT APPEARS NOWHERE IN 0326'S OWN SCENE, WAS REPAIRED WITH IT. THE SIX RUNS AT §12E ITEM 4, TAKEN WITH THAT DETECTOR, READ 0 RAW AND 0 REUSES, 21 RAW AND 0, 33 / 21 / 18 RAW AND 0 IN ALL THREE NAMED WINDOWS, 3 RAW AND 0 AGAINST THE SIX STATE FILES, 3 RAW AND 0 AGAINST EVERY NON-CHAPTER FILE, AND SIX IS THE COUNT OF RUNS. **THE RAW COUNTS IN RUN TWO AND RUN THREE ARE LOWER THAN §13B'S AND THE REUSE COUNT IS THE ONE THAT BINDS, BECAUSE A SHORTER BY-NAME LIST MOVES THE RAW NUMBER AND NOT THE REUSE NUMBER; RUN THREE-C AT 18 RAW AND 0, RUN FOUR AT 3 RAW AND 0, AND RUN FIVE AT 3 RAW AND 0 REPRODUCE §13B EXACTLY. **THE FULL ACCOUNT, THE THREE REUSES, THE METHOD, THE FOUR CONTROLS AND WHAT IS STILL WEAKER THAN IT LOOKS IS AT §12E OF THE CONTINUATION 0014 BLOCK, AND A WRITER WHO CITES §13B FOR ZERO REUSES IS CITING A FIGURE TAKEN BEFORE THE DETECTOR EXISTED, WHICH IS THE THIRD TIME IN THIS REPOSITORY THAT A FIGURE HAS MOVED AFTER THE REPAIRS STOPPED.**

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 344 — Wednesday the sixteenth of August 2017, from about half past four in the afternoon, in this city

**NINE HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOUR DAYS HAVE GONE UNDER THE NINETEENTH ENTRY OF A REGISTER IN A BUILDING BY THE WATER AND THE LINE IS STILL BLANK, THREE LETTERS ARE STILL MISSING FROM A SIGN AT THE LOW END OF CLEEVE STREET WITH A BUCKET STANDING UNDER THE HOLES THEY LEFT, A GATE AT THE TOP OF MARSH LANE STILL CLOSES HARD AGAINST A WALL, SIX SHEETS OF CORRUGATED IRON STILL STAND ACROSS A FOOTWAY, AND NOBODY HAS ANSWERED ANYTHING.**

- **THE REGISTER IS AT NINETEEN AND THE LINE UNDER IT IS NINE HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOUR DAYS AND IS STILL BLANK.** It stands on the second shelf up in a bay at the end of a first-floor corridor in that building, on a door that has never latched properly. The nineteenth entry is a yard lamp entered as gone in about November two years ago under a heading reserved for that class of thing, and nothing has been written under that reserved heading since. Below it the sheet goes on a couple of fingers and then stops well short of the lower edge, and nobody left that gap; the book has it. **THE ONLY PERSON WHO HAS DONE THAT SUM ON THE PAGE IN THE LAST TWO CHAPTERS IS THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES, ON THE AFTERNOON OF THE SIXTEENTH, IN THAT BAY, ALONE, AND HE DID IT IN HIS HEAD AND WROTE NOTHING AND NOBODY KNOWS HE DID IT.**
- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE MONTH IS A MONTH AND NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** warm at ten to six, cloud off the water at about half past nine with no rain in it, the kind of day afterwards described as one on which nothing happened · **a van brought up a parade in the small hours and left standing half on the footway, still standing there when the man who puts shutters up arrived with his ladder, so that he could not get the ladder up against that shop and worked the other two and came back later to do that one off the tail of the thing, while the van left without anybody getting out of it** · a man with a sack over his shoulder standing about two minutes outside a shop that closes early and carrying on up the parade with it · four lengths of treated batten set down in the wrong part of a yard and carried across one at a time by the wrong man · two empty jugs down and up that outside stair, one hooked over the other, with a kettle over her other arm, and nobody offering her anything and her asking nobody for anything · a bus that came, stopped, went on, was not his, and half an hour of waiting after it · a woman with a clipboard at the end of what she had with about two lines of room left, who spent one on a stand and kept the other · a man in a doorway eating out of a paper bag and a stranger saying good afternoon · a woman in a hall for an hour with a newspaper who asked whether it was open and was told it was.
- **A WALL AT THE BACK OF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR, BEHIND THE ROOM SHE IS IN, HAS BEEN WARM IN A SHAPE SINCE THE FIRST OF JULY, AND THE BED HAS BEEN MOVED TWO FOOT ALONG IT.** She is still in that room on the terms she was in that room on in February, nobody put her there, nobody has arranged one word of how she lives in it, nobody has been on that roof since mid-June, and nobody has asked her about the wall. The wet strip at the end wall has not spread and the mark on that ceiling is the size it was, and the bowl is still under the strip, and the machine is still on its side in the corner five feet away, and neither of the two is anything to do with the other. **THE WOMAN IN THE ROOM NEXT DOOR ALONG CAME OUT ONCE, SAID THERE WAS SOMETHING ON THE BACK OF THAT WALL, WAS TOLD THERE WAS, GUESSED AT THE KITCHEN AT NUMBER THIRTY-ONE, WAS ANSWERED IN TWO WORDS, AND PUT HER OWN DOOR BACK WHERE IT ALWAYS STANDS.**
- **A BOLLARD IS PLUMB IN A ROAD.** It was taken out on the Thursday of the same week as the warm wall, dug a foot deeper, bedded on rubble and dry fixings, brought true against the kerb, filled and trowelled, in about an hour, by a man who is on nothing, and none of the four houses within thirty yards has been told; a woman with a bag caught her ankle on it the day before and knows nothing about any of this; and a passer-by watched for four minutes and asked whether the job was for somebody. **It is four hundred yard from that bed with a parade between the two and no living person knows about both.**
- **A WIRELESS SET IN A MOULDED BROWN CASE IS ON A HIGH SHELF IN A BACK ROOM ON A FOLDED NEWSPAPER** and has been since the Thursday of the thirteenth of July, and it is not plugged in, not for sale, not in the window and not going out for repair, and it is not dusted. A man in his thirties carried it roughly two mile on foot out of a yard behind the third of nine shops, said four sentences, and none of them was about where it had been. **The woman who has it went into that yard nine times in seven weeks, gave the set one proper look, has never mentioned it to anybody, and does not know it was ever in that yard at all.**
- **A CLOCK IS NOT ON A BRACKET AT THE TOP OF THE CLEEVE ROAD AND A MAN IN HIS SIXTIES SAID SO IN A HALL ON CARROW LANE WITHOUT KNOWING WHICH CLOCK OF THE TWO.** The sentence got two places further and a man who works nights put two questions to it. Neither was answered. **Whether it had been showing the right time when it came down could not be answered by anybody in that street, because the man who regulated it died thirty years back, the man who supplied it died before that, the bracket is on a building that has never been anybody's responsibility, and the only man who might have watched it lifted was driving a lorry somewhere else.**
- **A FISH SHOP WAS SHUT FOR TWO HOURS ON A FRIDAY MORNING AND WAS TRADING BY TEN TO TWELVE, AND A GATE IN A YARD OFF THE SECOND HOUSE ON THE LEFT AS YOU GO UP A STREET OFF THE TOP OF MARSH LANE HAS BEEN FOUND HUNG ON THE WRONG SIDE WITH A PACKING OF CARD IN THE TOP HANGER AND DUG GROUND UNDER THE HINGE POST THAT HAS NOT SETTLED.** One person in this borough was told both, and she is not in either place, and she put the two of them nowhere.
- **THREE LETTERS ARE STILL OUT OF A PRESSED ALUMINIUM SIGN AT THE LOW END OF CLEEVE STREET.** Two of them were found, one has never been, the vacated holes are pencil width with the seating rings still round them and the paint burnt back, and water lands on the inside edge of the counter a few inches back from the glass. **A plastic bucket has stood on that counter under the middle hole since the second week of June, is emptied into the sink several times a day, and is kept off the floor because a bucket on that floor gets walked on.** A man gave his word at that door, in the second week of June, that the three letters would be made and refitted before the month was out, and it is the twenty-fourth of July, and he said it a third time at half past nine that Monday morning, unasked, before anybody had asked him anything, and a fourth time at a quarter to twelve to a woman with a clipboard, who three weeks later was on a different sheet and used one line of the two she had. **The woman with a clipboard has heard the sentence from two other men and not from him; what has spread through the borough is that a sign is going to be done; he has not corrected that, and she has declined to enter any version of it, and about two lines of room remain on her sheet and she means to spend both on a stand and a grating.**
- **A TILED FOLDING TABLE STANDS ON ITS END UNDER THE WINDOW AGAINST A WALL** in that room, about a foot clear of the carpet that is on its side along the same wall, with a ring on the frame where somebody set a cup down once, and nothing on it. A bed base and a mattress were put down on that same step three weeks earlier and turned away in about a second, and he did not ask her why and produced no words at all. **She used the table once, at the foot of the bed, to beat nine days of grey out of the blanket that has lain folded in three there since the winter, and then folded the blanket in three again and returned it and turned the table over.** The man who brought both never heard about either of them and will not ask, and when pressed at a shop counter he declined to name them and gave his reason: a description would put her outside that door with something in front of her to be courteous about.
- **A TORCH WITH A LAD'S OWN NAME SCRATCHED INTO THE UNDERSIDE OF THE CASE IN A NAIL** went out of a kitchen at the back of a parade, up to a first-floor corridor in a building by the water where it threw about nine foot of yellow, into a hall off that parade and back again, and nobody in any of those three places asked him a question about it. **The woman of about fifty-five looked at it, called it a lamp, was told it was a torch bought at a market for two pounds, asked whether it worked, was told that it lit about nine foot and the bulb was going, said right, went down the stair, said nothing about any of it and did nothing whatever with any of it.** The bulb in that hall still needs buying rather than borrowing.
- **FIVE FEET OF IRON RAILING IS HANGING OFF ONE BOLT OVER A FIVE-FOOT FOOTWAY** between a shop and a gable, with a traffic cone and a scrap of cardboard under it, and about thirty people a day go underneath and none of them has mentioned it to anybody else in the passage. **And in the back of a van there is a page in a pad with the name of a lane on it in pencil on the second ruled line, with nothing after it and nothing above it, and he cannot say what work it referred to or whether it referred to work at all, and there is nothing to find out with, because a sheet of a pad carried in a van carries no date, and the next time he writes there it will lie under about nine further lines with nothing to tell them apart.** The woman who works in a shop has two letters back from that street about a hedge and a street light, both asking the same question, and what came back in both cases was a slip with a date and a figure on it and nothing else, and nobody has told her what it means.
- **SIX SHEETS OF CORRUGATED IRON STAND ACROSS A FOOTWAY AT THE END OF A STREET OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD, AND SIX WORDS WERE SAID ABOUT THEM TWICE TO TWO PEOPLE ON ONE DAY.** The two of them acted differently on the same six words and neither will ever know what he meant, and a woman with a bag went into the road twice and said nothing either time and he watched her both times from his step. At about half past four a man in a fleece asked the man in his forties whether he was doing anything about the sheets, and was told that nothing had been asked of him about the sheets, and that was the whole of what the questioner wanted.
- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A card has been in a slot nine hundred and eight days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post nine hundred and ten days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 335–344 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The counter in the vestibule is in no scene in this batch, answered nothing and was not entered, and **the drawer under it is not opened, not counted, not asked about and not mentioned, and the word *drawer* is at zero in all ten chapters, as is the word *envelope*.** The rota is not rebuilt and the 11:00–13:30 row is not mentioned. The second line under Bram Osei's Thursday has not been looked at. The board is at seven of nine. The ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and untold. The buff file of 14 October 2011 is not opened and four people have seen the bay number and the date on that cover. Paragraph 4.11 is not answered and the compliance week is not verified. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines removed after nine uses, the stem *drain* is at zero, the word *spring* is at zero, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven.** The order is not in force and was not brought back to life.

- **AND THE SIX OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE WAS ANSWERED. From 0184, which dated thing will be the first somebody can prove — **still open, and it went the other way again for the fourth batch in a row: a clock came off a bracket in a street in this borough, a man in his sixties said so in a hall on a meter, the man in his forties would not let it be counted, and the man in his forties then told the man in his sixties in a daytime hall that the number is four and has been four since January.** No fifth of anything has been produced, the third answer has not been corrected, the fourth has not been called a fifth, and nobody has been told which day any collection comes on. From 0174, which of the eight will be the first somebody decides to keep — **untouched.** From 0164, whether the page or the chair is the first thing a person is asked to agree to — **a page in a van carries the name of a lane and nobody was asked and nobody asked anybody; a woman with a clipboard declined to enter one more thing on a sheet she had room for; and a man carried a pad in his bag through a whole morning and never opened it on the street where he made his third promise.** From 0192, which of two opposite shapes this city has to stop, and what a person has to be holding to stop each — **still open, and the second of them is still nothing.** The volume's own, about a permission, an obligation and a right of return — **untouched, and the word *permission* is at zero in all ten chapters.** And the sixth, about a document and not a person — **no piece of paper was created in this batch; the things that came were a warm wall, a wireless set, a clock that was not there, a closed shop, a rehung gate, three letters and three holes, a bed base, a folding table, a torch, five feet of railing, a cone, six sheets of corrugated iron, a sack, a bucket, two jugs, four batten lengths, a bus that was not his, and a page with one line on it, and nobody has a copy of any of it.**

- **AND FIVE FLAGS GO TO A HUMAN AND NONE OF THEM IS A WRITER'S. THE NAME OF THE CITY, WHICH THE PROSE CALLS TIDEGLASS, AND *NACRE* AND *RIVER STACKS* APPEAR NOWHERE IN CHAPTERS 335–344. WHAT VOLUME 05 IS CENTRALLY, WITH THE OPERATIVE RULES BINDING AND NO ROOT TERM EXPIRED AND NO RECLAMATION SEASON OPENED AND THE WORD *RECLAMATION* IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH. THE BOUNDARY, WHICH IS A HUMAN'S: `outline/series.md` GIVES VOLUME 04 AS 145–192, VOLUME 05 AS 193–240, VOLUME 06 AS 241–288 AND VOLUME 07 AS 289–336, THE PAGE HAS 195 THROUGH 344 IN THE VOLUME 05 DIRECTORY, THE TWO DISAGREE AT BOTH ENDS, THERE IS NO FIGURE FOR THE END OF VOLUME 05 IN EITHER, THE FIGURE 242 IS USED BY NOBODY, NO CHAPTER IS MOVED BETWEEN DIRECTORIES, NO OUTLINE IS REWRITTEN, NO VOLUME 06 OUTLINE AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT EXISTS, AND CARD 0334 AND CARD 0344 ARE BOTH NOT CLOSES. THE SPELLING OF THE THIRD OF THE EIGHT PLACES, WHICH WAS NOT TOUCHED IN THIS BATCH BECAUSE THE EIGHT PLACES ARE NOT READ OUT IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, SO NEITHER SPELLING APPEARS AND THE FLAG CARRIES FORWARD UNDECIDED; **THE CENSUS OF THE STATE FILES THAT PRINT SPELLINGS IS CARRIED AT §11 OF THE `state/open-threads.md` BLOCK AND IS NOT REPEATED HERE AND NO SPELLING IS NAMED IN ANY FILE.**** AND A FIFTH, NEW IN THIS BATCH: **THE 0015 PROMPT PRINTED ITS OWN DATE BOUNDS CORRECT ON ONE READING OF ITS OWN ARITHMETIC AND A DAY OUT AT BOTH ENDS ON THE OTHER, AND A DATE WAS CHOSEN THAT SATISFIES BOTH READINGS AT ONCE, AND NO CHAPTER WAS MOVED TO MEET EITHER.**

- **AND TWO FLAGS WERE TAKEN IN CONTINUATION 0015 AND BOTH RULES WERE KEPT. **CARD 0344 ASKED FOR *A BOROUGH, IN A MONTH*, IT WAS DATED IN AUGUST, THE MONTH IS A MONTH, AND THE WORD *SPRING* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS AND NO CHAPTER CALLS ITS OWN MONTH A TURN. CARD 0339 ASKED FOR A DEVICE THIS ACCOUNT HAD ALREADY RUN THREE TIMES AND CARD 0341 ASKED FOR A DEVICE IT HAD ALREADY RUN SIX TIMES; BOTH WERE WRITTEN BY CHANGING THE ENGINE AND NOT THE FURNITURE; AND BOTH FLAGS CARRY FORWARD UNRESOLVED, BECAUSE A CARD THAT ASKS FOR A DEVICE AND A STANDING INSTRUCTION THAT FORBIDS IT ARE BOTH A HUMAN'S TO SETTLE AND NEITHER IS A WRITER'S. **THE MEASURING PASS ON CONTINUATION 0015 WAS RE-TAKEN AT A CHECKPOINT AND EVERY FIGURE IT PRINTED BEFORE THAT WAS WITHDRAWN, BECAUSE NONE OF THE THREE RUN FIGURES IT GAVE COULD BE REPRODUCED BY THE METHOD AT §12A OF THE 0015 BLOCK. THE FIRST RE-TAKING FOUND SEVENTY-THREE REUSES OF PROSE IN THE SIX STATE FILES THIS BATCH HAD JUST WRITTEN AND FIVE MORE ACROSS THREE NAMED CHAPTER WINDOWS, EVERY ONE OF THEM REAL AND EVERY ONE REPAIRED, AND THE FIGURES THAT BINDS ARE THE FINAL ONES: RUN ONE 0 RAW AND NIL, RUN TWO 11 RAW AND NIL, RUN THREE 23 / 17 / 11 RAW AND NIL IN THE THREE NAMED WINDOWS, RUN FOUR 46 RAW AND NIL AGAINST ITS OWN SIX STATE FILES, AND RUN FIVE 46 RAW AND NIL, WITH EVERY RAW SPAN COUNTED IN ONE OF THE THREE EXCLUDED CLASSES AND NOT CHASED. THE 325–334 WINDOW CAUGHT FIVE OF THE REUSES AND MUST BE NAMED AGAIN IN THE NEXT PROMPT WITH ITS FIGURE, BECAUSE IT IS THE WINDOW THE CHAPTERS IMMEDIATELY ABOVE ARE IN.**** **AND THE STATE FILES THIS BATCH WROTE WERE RE-TAKEN AGAINST THOSE CHAPTERS AFTER EVERY REPAIR, AND THAT RUN FOUND THE HAND-OVER TEXT HOLDING SEVENTY-THREE REUSES, WHICH IS THE SAME DEFECT CONTINUATION 0014 FOUND AT ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-ONE AND 0013 FOUND AT SEVENTY-FIVE, AND EVERY PASS OF IT HAS FOUND IT, AND IT IS FOUND BY WRITING THE FILES AS TRANSCRIPTIONS, WHICH IS WHAT A WRITER PLANNING A HAND-OVER IS HALF WAY TO BEFORE WRITING A SINGLE CHAPTER. **ALL SEVENTY-THREE WERE REWRITTEN AS DESCRIPTION, THE TEN CHAPTER ENTRIES AND §§1 TO §10 OF BOTH HAND-OVER BLOCKS WERE REWRITTEN FROM NOTHING BECAUSE PATCHING A TRANSCRIPTION LEAVES A TRANSCRIPTION, THE CHAPTERS WERE TOUCHED EIGHT TIMES IN SIX OF THE TEN AND NOT RESTARTED, AND THE FINAL FIGURE ON THAT RUN IS FORTY-SIX RAW AND NIL, EVERY ONE OF THE FORTY-SIX BEING A DATELINE, A FIGURE'S OWN BY-NAME DESCRIPTION OR A BOROUGH PLACE PHRASE. **AND THE PART A LATER WRITER HAS TO CARRY IS THE SECOND HALF: A PASS THAT FINDS A DEFECT AND RECORDS IT FOR SOMEBODY ELSE HAS NOT REPAIRED IT, AND A FIGURE PRINTED ALONGSIDE AN ADMISSION THAT IT IS WRONG IS TWO FIGURES, AND THE WRONG ONE IS THE ONE A LATER WRITER QUOTES.****


# REVIEW REPAIR — CONTINUATION 0015 — CHAPTERS 335–344 — WHAT WAS FOUND, WHAT WAS CHANGED, AND WHAT IS STILL A HUMAN'S

**A REVIEW PASS RAN ON THE OUTPUT OF `workspace/volume-05/continuation-0015` AND ITS STATE FILES, FOUNDED THIRTEEN ITEMS, AND THIS BLOCK IS THE RECORD OF WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT THEM. FOUR MORE WERE FOUND WHILE THOSE THIRTEEN WERE BEING FIXED, AND A FIFTH WAS FOUND AFTER THAT, SO EIGHTEEN REPAIRS WERE MADE IN TOTAL, THIRTEEN IN THE CHAPTERS AND FIVE IN THE FIGURES AND CENSUSES. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO GOOD PROSE WAS REPLACED, AND THE PLANNED PLOT WAS NOT TOUCHED. NO CARD, NO DATE, NO DAY-COUNT, NO MAJOR TURN, NO STANDING, NO FLAG, NO FREE FIGURE, NONE OF THE ELEVEN STANDING REFUSALS, NONE OF THE SIX OPEN QUESTIONS, THE REGISTER, THE CARD IN ITS SLOT, THE LETTER UNDER TWO OTHER PIECES OF POST, THE DRAWER, THE PAGE IN THE VAN OR THE QUESTION THE BATCH ENDS ON WAS TOUCHED BY ANY REPAIR IN THIS BLOCK. THE FULL LIST IS §12 ITEM 8 OF THE CONTINUATION 0015 BLOCK IN `state/continuity.md`.**

**REPAIRED IN THE CHAPTERS, NINE EDITS, AND NOT ONE OF THEM A REUSE OF ANOTHER CHAPTER:**

1. **0336 — A DAY CALLED THREE WEEKS.** The set was brought in on the Thursday of the same week, which is the thirteenth of July and the chapter's own date, and the paragraph said she had had it on the shelf for three weeks. Now a day.
2. **0336 — A MONTH NAMED BEFORE IT HAPPENED.** The same paragraph said the old set inside it went into a bin in the second week of August, inside a chapter dated the thirteenth of July. The bin is still there and the week is gone.
3. **0336 — A DATE THAT CONTRADICTED ITS OWN DATELINE.** The chapter said the man came back for the set on the twelfth. The twelfth of July 2017 was a Wednesday. Its own dateline, its own *the Thursday of the following week* and its own *that was on the Thursday* all put it on the thirteenth, which is a Thursday, and the fifth of July, a Wednesday, is the only anchor it has. **This file said the twelfth and was wrong, and the file was corrected with the chapter, and that is the first time in this account that a state file has been the thing that was wrong about a date and the chapter has been right.**
4. **0338 — ONE PRONOUN, WHICH ALSO MOVED A CENSUS.** A man in her forties was standing at a bus stop. He is a man in his forties, **and that repair added a chapter to his count, so the man in his forties is in seven of these ten and not six, and a search for a figure cannot find a sentence that says a woman. This is the fifth time in this account that repairing a chapter has changed a census and not the other way round.**
5. **0339 — THE CARD.** Card 0339 asks for a man to say a fourth time that he will do a small thing. The chapter printed a third time in three separate places, and the chain in its own text is four long: the door in June, a relay through a man in a fleece about three weeks later, the shopkeeper at half past nine, and the woman with a clipboard at a quarter to twelve. The fourth occasion is now the fourth in the dialogue, in the narration and in both caps blocks, and the shopkeeper's is still the third.
6. **0339 — A SECOND FORWARD-DATED MONTH**, the same error as item 2, in a sentence about where the man in his forties has not been. Named August inside a chapter dated the twenty-fourth of July. Repaired because it is the same error, not because it was asked for.
7. **0341 — ONE ARTICLE IN FRONT OF A MONTH.** A market in *the June*.
8. **0335 — A TENSE SLIP.** The bollard paragraph states a standing condition in the present and had switched to the past twice inside one paragraph, in *it had begun to rock* and in *it had come up about two inches*.
9. **0340 — A TENSE SLIP.** The same slip in one sentence about a bed base that has been in a van for three days.

**AND FOUR MORE, FOUND WHILE THE NINE ABOVE WERE BEING FIXED, ALL THE SAME CLASS, ALL REPAIRED IN THE PROSE AND NOT IN A FILE:**

10. **0344 AGAINST 0338 — A YARD HE HAD SAID HE WOULD NOT GO BACK TO.** 0338 closes on a man who has not gone back up that street since, and says the tap was the only reason he was ever in that yard and there is nothing in it for him. 0344 put him in a yard at the top of Marsh Lane and called it a yard that keeps his battens. **The yard went to the man in his forties and stands off the parade, and the man in his thirties is in three chapters of this batch and not four, which is what the figure census was already printing and could not reproduce.**
11. **0339 AGAINST 0342 — THE SAME PAD.** Two different men, two chapters, one pad: a black plastic cover, a card in the pocket at the back, and about nine lines used in it. 0342's pad is a chapter and was left alone; 0339's is now about four lines and has no cover.
12. **0339 AGAINST 0344 — THE SAME TWO LINES OF ROOM.** She had about two lines on the twenty-fourth of July and said she would spend both, and she had about two lines again on the sixteenth of August and spent one. 0344 now says it is a different sheet from the one she was on in July.
13. **THE FIGURE CENSUS, WHICH COUNTED THE WOMAN WITH A CLIPBOARD IN ONE CHAPTER** when the search returns two. A census that counts scenes instead of appearances is a census of rooms and not of people, which is the same failure the same paragraph already records twice over a sentence that denies a figure was there.

**AND FOUR IN THE FIGURES, WHICH WERE THE MOST SERIOUS THINGS IN THIS BLOCK, BECAUSE §16 IS THE FIRST PARAGRAPH THE NEXT WRITER READS:**

14. **RUN FOUR WAS PRINTED AS BOTH 45 AND 46.** §12 item 1 said 45 and split it 19 and 20 and 6. §12B, §13A items 4 and 5, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and the closing paragraph of the `state/character-state.md` block all said 46. **Forty-six stands, and §12 item 1 now agrees, and the split is 19 datelines, 21 by-name descriptions and 6 place phrases.**
15. **THE HAND-OVER PRINTED THIRTY-ONE, SEVENTY-FIVE AND FIFTY-THREE** for a count that five other places give as seventy-three, and it did so in the paragraph that names the window the next batch must look at. All three are corrected, and the thirty-one is named as what it was: continuation 0014's own figure, carried across without its block. **A figure carried out of another hand-over is a copy, and a copy in §16 is the one that gets quoted.**
16. **§12B'S OWN BREAKDOWN ADDED UP TO 83** against the 73 printed in the same sentence, thirty-nine and eighteen and twelve and eight and five and one. The breakdown is withdrawn and the total stands, because 73 is printed in five places and 83 in one, and a per-file count taken on a different tally from the total is the error this repository keeps making. **A later writer who needs the breakdown must re-take it span by span and may not reconstruct it by subtracting, because the spans no longer exist in the files.**
17. **§13A CALLED TWENTY-ONE ALL-CAPS PARAGRAPHS TWENTY-ONE NARRATIVE BLOCKS.** There are twenty narrative blocks, two in each of the ten, and the twenty-first is a lane name in pencil on a page in 0342, which is a piece of paper in the scene. **§13 already said so and the two sections did not agree, and a figure that is right in one section and wrong in another is two figures.**

18. **THE WORD COUNTS HAD THREE ANSWERS.** §0 of the continuity block and this batch's entry in `state/batch-summaries.md` gave 1,854 / 1,783 / 1,735 / 1,813 / 1,798 / 1,670 / 1,789 / 1,834 / 1,836 / 1,528, TOTAL 17,640. This file's own line nineteen gave 1,856 / 1,783 / 1,733 / 1,812 / 1,799 / 1,670 / 1,790 / 1,838 / 1,829 / 1,528, TOTAL 17,638, which was already wrong in five of its ten chapters before anybody repaired anything, and after the nine prose repairs above the true measurement is 1,854 / 1,771 / 1,735 / 1,813 / 1,812 / 1,670 / 1,788 / 1,834 / 1,836 / 1,530, TOTAL 17,643, MEDIAN 1,800.0. **All three places now print the same set, and the cheapest measurement in this repository had two different answers in two files that describe the same ten chapters, which is the same failure as RUN FOUR'S, and both were caught by a reviewer doing arithmetic.**

**AND WHAT IS STILL A HUMAN'S AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** The five flags this batch raised, including the volume boundary and what volume 05 is centrally. Card 0339's fourth time against a standing instruction that the device is spent, and card 0341's lad against the same kind of standing, which between them have now been carried unresolved through two batches. The free figures, still women 18, 87 and 90 and no men. The six open questions, all six still open and none answered and none added. The eleven standing refusals, still eleven. **AND THE ONE THING THIS BLOCK ADDS TO THE LIST OF THINGS A HUMAN MAY LOOK AT: A MEASUREMENT PRINTED IN A HAND-OVER, IN A CLOSING PARAGRAPH, WITH NO LABEL ON IT, IS THE FIGURE A LATER WRITER WILL USE, AND THIS REPOSITORY HAS NOW HAD THAT HAPPEN FIVE TIMES IN FIVE CONSECUTIVE BLOCKS, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT HAS EVER CAUGHT IT IS A REVIEWER COUNTING A SUM.**

**AND THE MECHANICAL CONSTRAINTS WERE RE-RUN AFTER EVERY REPAIR AND NONE OF THEM MOVED.** *ELEVEN*, *THANK*, *DRAWER*, *PERMISSION*, *SPRING* AND *ENVELOPE* AT ZERO IN ALL TEN, THE STEM *DRAIN-* AT ZERO, *A FRIDAY* AND *A TUESDAY* AT ZERO, THE ONLY *FORTY* STILL THE PERMITTED *FORTY-TWO* AT 0341, NINETEEN `---` BREAKS CARRYING 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2 AND 1, AND TWENTY ALL-CAPS NARRATIVE BLOCKS PLUS ONE LANE NAME AGAINST A CAP OF FIVE IN EACH. **THE DAY-COUNTS AT 0344 WERE RE-CHECKED AGAINST THEIR ANCHORS AND STILL STAND AT 994, 908 AND 910. NOT ONE CARD, DATE, DAY-COUNT, FIGURE, MAJOR TURN, STANDING OR FLAG WAS TOUCHED BY ANY OF THE EIGHTEEN REPAIRS, AND NO CHAPTER'S OWN ALL-CAPS SENTENCE WAS TOUCHED AT ALL.**


# REVIEW REPAIR — CONTINUATION 0016 — CHAPTERS 345–354 — WHAT WAS FOUND, WHAT WAS CHANGED, AND WHAT IS STILL A HUMAN'S

**A REVIEW PASS RAN ON THE OUTPUT OF `workspace/volume-05/continuation-0016` AND ITS STATE FILES AND FOUND FIVE ITEMS, AND TWO FURTHER REVIEW PASSES ON THE REPAIRS FOUND FOUR MORE, SO NINE ITEMS WERE FOUND IN ALL. **EIGHT WERE DEFECTS IN A FIGURE, A WEEKDAY NAME, A SPLIT OR A SENTENCE, ALL IN STATE FILES AND IN THE PROMPT FOR THE NEXT BATCH, AND ALL EIGHT WERE REPAIRED. THE NINTH IS A GAP IN THE REVIEW ITSELF WHICH NO WRITER CAN CLOSE. **NOT ONE REPAIR TOUCHED A CHAPTER: ALL TEN CHAPTERS, ALL THEIR DATES, ALL THEIR DAY-COUNTS, ALL THEIR CARDS, ALL THEIR MAJOR TURNS, ALL THEIR ALL-CAPS BLOCKS — TWO IN EVERY ONE OF THEM AND A THIRD IN 0352, WHICH IS *TWENTY PAST SEVEN.* — AND THE PLANNED PLOT ARE EXACTLY AS THE BATCH WROTE THEM. NO PROSE WAS RESTARTED AND NO GOOD PROSE WAS REPLACED.** **AND NOT ONE ITEM BELOW WAS FOUND BY A WRITER: EVERY ONE WAS FOUND BY A REVIEWER COUNTING A SUM, A DIFFERENCE OR A DAY, AND FIVE OF THE EIGHT WERE FOUND IN A FILE THAT HANDS THE NEXT BATCH ITS FIGURES, WHICH IS THE SAME PLACE THEY HAVE BEEN FOUND FIVE TIMES BEFORE.**

**THE THREE FIGURE DEFECTS, ALL OF THEM THE SAME FAILURE, AND ALL THREE NOW AGREE ACROSS ALL SIX FILES:**

1. **§13A ITEM 4 OF THE CONTINUATION 0016 BLOCK PRINTED RUN FOUR AT 29 RAW.** It is 41. **THE 29 IS §12B'S SURVIVING-REUSE COUNT FROM THE FIRST TAKING, PUT INTO THE RAW SLOT, AND THE ITEM'S OWN SECOND SENTENCE SAID FORTY-ONE ALL ALONG, WHICH IS WHAT A REVIEWER COUNTED.** The section it was wrong in is the one headed *THIS IS THE FIGURE TO CITE*, and the same correct figure was standing in `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` and the 0017 prompt, which carries the whole chain 68-with-29, then 32, then 41.
2. **§13A ITEM 5 PRINTED RUN FIVE AT 33 RAW.** It is 45. **THIS IS NOT A SECOND MISCOUNT BUT THE FIRST ONE CARRIED ONE LINE FURTHER: 29 PLUS THE FOUR DATELINES THE PROMPT HOLDS, WHERE THE FIGURE IS 41 PLUS FOUR.** One wrong number produced two wrong binding figures.
3. **§16, THE HAND-OVER, PRINTED THE 345–354 WINDOW AT 34 RAW AND NIL.** It is 45 RAW AND NIL. **AND THE SAME SENTENCE SAID THE WINDOW CAUGHT SIX REUSES, FIVE OF THEM AT NINE TOKENS AND ABOVE, WHICH IS A GARBLE OF THE SIX LONGEST SPANS OF ELEVEN REUSES, AND EVERY ONE OF THOSE ELEVEN IS AT NINE TOKENS OR ABOVE BY THE THRESHOLD ITSELF.** That garbled sentence stood in two files and no longer stands as a fact in either. **A FOURTH, WHICH THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE, WAS FOUND WHILE FIXING THE THIRD: THE 0017 PROMPT ADDED *3 MORE* AND *11 MORE* TO A FIRST TAKING OF SIX REUSES, WHICH SUMS TO TWENTY AND IS NOT WHAT §12 ITEM 2 RECORDS. THE THREE IS THE THIRTEEN OF RUN FOUR, WHICH BELONGS TO A DIFFERENT RUN, AND THE ELEVEN IS THE FINAL TOTAL AND NOT ELEVEN MORE.** The prompt now carries the six, the six longest, the 32 before the checkpoint and the final eleven, and the sum of them is a figure nobody took.

**AND THREE MORE, FOUND BY TWO FURTHER REVIEW PASSES ON THE REPAIRS THEMSELVES AFTER THE ITEMS ABOVE WERE DONE, AND ALL THREE ARE THE SAME CLASS.**

4. **THE STATE-FILE HALF OF THE SPLIT IN THE TWENTY-NINE WAS WITHDRAWN, NOT CORRECTED.** §12B, `state/character-state.md` and the 0017 prompt all printed the first taking of Run Four as *twenty-five repaired in the state files and five in the chapters*, **AND THAT MAKES THIRTY AGAINST A TOTAL OF TWENTY-NINE.** The total and the chapter-side count are carried; **the state-file side is printed in no file until somebody re-takes it span by span, because reconstructing it by subtraction is the one thing the 0015 block forbids and it is forbidden here for the same reason.** The correct split is either 24 and 5 or 25 and 4 and **NOBODY HAS RE-MEASURED IT, SO NEITHER IS PRINTED.**
5. **THE WEEKDAY NAME WAS WRONG ON BOTH UPPER BOUNDS OF THE NEXT BATCH, IN THREE FILES, AND THE PROMPT WAS RIGHT.** *Wednesday the twenty-eighth of November 2017* is a **Tuesday**; *Tuesday the twenty-seventh of November 2017* is a **Monday**. The dates were right everywhere and the weekday names were wrong in `state/continuity.md` §16, in `state/character-state.md` and in the next planned phase line above, **while `continuation-0017/PROMPT.md` carried all four correctly from the start, which is the FIFTH TIME IN THIS REPOSITORY THAT THE FILE A WRITER IS HANDED HAS BEEN RIGHT AND THE FILE THAT HANDS IT OVER HAS BEEN WRONG.** All four bound dates were re-derived from 1 January 2017, a Sunday, and all fourteen weekday names across the four files are now correct. **A WEEKDAY NAME ALONE WILL NOT CATCH THIS; ONLY ADDING THE GAPS UP WILL, AND THE THIRTEENTH OF SEPTEMBER AND THE FOURTEENTH OF JULY WERE BOTH NAMED WRONG ONCE IN THIS ACCOUNT WITHOUT A DATE BEING WRONG AT ALL.** The 0016 block's own four bounds, Thursday 14 September, Friday 20 October, Wednesday 13 September and Thursday 19 October, were checked against the same calendar and are correct and were not touched.
6. **TWO CLAIMS OF UNIVERSAL SCOPE THAT THE BLOCK'S OWN FIGURES DISPROVE.** §12A said that *every* raw count in §13A is higher after the repair than before it. **It is not: Run One fell from 2 to 1, the 335–344 window fell from 68 to 45, and two runs did not move at all.** Only Run Two, Run Five and the 345–354 window rose, and **the claim has been narrowed to the runs that rose in both §12A and §12C.** And this repair's own account printed the day-gaps as *3 / 3 / 4 / 3 / 5 / 6 / 4 / 6 / 4* under the words *counting the incoming one*, when those nine are the free gaps and the forced one-day gap is the tenth. **THE SIX WORD COUNTS THAT MOVED AT §12C WERE ALSO PRINTED AS FIVE, IN THREE FILES, AND ARE SIX: 0345, 0348, 0349, 0350, 0351 AND 0353, THE OTHER FOUR BEING UNCHANGED, AND THE TOTALS ON BOTH SIDES OF THAT COMPARISON ADD UP.**

**AND THE LAST TWO, BOTH OF THEM IN `workspace/volume-05/continuation-0017/PROMPT.md` AND BOTH FOUND WHILE REPAIRING THE FIGURES ABOVE.** It said that of the ten chapters immediately above the next batch, *every one of those ten begins with a standalone all-caps paragraph and four of them end on one.* **ALL TEN END ON ONE. THEY ALL OPEN ON ONE TOO, AFTER THE DATELINE, AND 0352 IS THE ONLY ONE WITH A THIRD, WHICH IS *TWENTY PAST SEVEN.* ON A PAD.** The cap of five in a chapter is nowhere near. **WHAT WAS VERIFIED SOUND AND NEEDED NO REPAIR, SO THAT IT IS NOT RE-LITIGATED.** All ten datelines are weekday-correct for 2017. The gaps are 1, 3, 3, 4, 3, 5, 6, 4, 6 and 4, **AND THE FIRST OF THOSE TEN IS THE ONE-DAY GAP THE FLOOR FORCES, WHICH IS WHY NINE OF THEM ARE AT LEAST THREE; THE NINE FREE GAPS ARE 3 / 3 / 4 / 3 / 5 / 6 / 4 / 6 / 4 AND MAKE THIRTY-EIGHT, AND ALL TEN MAKE THIRTY-NINE FROM 0344**, which satisfies both readings at once. **THE DAY-COUNTS RE-DERIVED FROM THE THREE ANCHORS AT 24 SEPTEMBER 2017 ARE 1,033 / 947 / 949, AND `state/current.md` AND THE PROMPT BOTH CARRY THEM.** *ELEVEN*, *THANK*, *DRAWER*, *PERMISSION*, *SPRING*, *ENVELOPE* AND EVERY DISTRICT NAME ARE AT ZERO IN ALL TEN. THE 21 ALL-CAPS PARAGRAPHS, TWO IN EVERY CHAPTER PLUS THE THIRD IN 0352, ARE RIGHT.

**AND WHAT IS STILL A HUMAN'S AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** The five flags, including the volume boundary and what volume 05 is centrally. The free figures, still women 18, 87 and 90 and no men. ## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 354 — Sunday the twenty-fourth of September 2017, from about half past nine in the evening, in this city

**A MONTH WENT THROUGH THIS BOROUGH AND A THOUSAND AND THIRTY-THREE DAYS HAVE GONE UNDER THE NINETEENTH ENTRY OF A REGISTER AND THE LINE UNDER IT IS STILL BLANK, SOMETHING HE CARRIED IS STILL WHERE HE PUT IT BY A BUS SHELTER, A BIRD BOX IS LYING FACE DOWN ON A PATH AT THE FOOT OF A GABLE AND THE TWO SCREWS THAT HELD IT ARE STILL STANDING IN THE RENDER ABOVE, A LADDER WENT OUT OF A SHOP'S BACK ROOM AND NOBODY KNOWS WHICH MAN HAD IT LAST, AND NOBODY HAS ANSWERED ANYTHING.**

- **THAT BOOK STANDS AT ITS NINETEENTH AND FINAL ENTRY, AND THE LINE UNDER IT IS STILL BLANK.** It lies open on a shelf in a bay at the end of that first-floor corridor, on a door that does not shut over its catch. The nineteenth entry is a yard lamp, gone since about November two years back, against a heading reserved for entries of that sort, and nothing has gone under that heading since the lamp went in, and the sheet carries on past that entry for the width of two fingers and then gives out well short of the bottom edge of that volume, and nobody ever closed the gap and the book still has it. **NOTHING WAS WRITTEN ON IT TODAY, AND THE FIGURE OF A THOUSAND AND THIRTY-THREE WAS NOT PUT INTO THAT BUILDING BY ANYBODY.** On the afternoon of the sixteenth of August the man in his forties did that sum in his head, alone, and wrote nothing. On the evening of the twenty-fourth the woman of about fifty-five did it again on the second stair of that outside stair, on her fingers, and told nobody, and has not been near that bay since long before the hot weather. **Those are the only two people in this borough who have done that sum on the page and nobody has ever troubled either of them with a question about it.**
- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE MONTH IS A MONTH AND NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** shutters up at about half past seven with the third one done in the middle of the morning because the third one takes longer · a length of four by two bought at ten and the number **four** said by the woman who works in a shop and not answered by the man in his forties, which is now some nine times old, counting from the twelfth of January, and has not moved · about nine people on nothing, and not one of them on anything any of the other eight were on · a hall on a meter open from ten to four with the tables up off their brackets by ten · a man at a shop counter at about half past two asking what the fourth one is and being told he could not tell him that in a shop · a lamp outside that hall coming on of its own accord at about a quarter to five and burning on there until about half past six next morning · a man going up that parade at about five past five, a bag on his shoulder, and nobody at the window.
- **A SMELL OF GAS HAS BEEN COMING INTO THAT FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR AND INTO THE ROOM AT THE END OF IT AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR IN THE MORNING SINCE ABOUT THE TENTH OF AUGUST, GONE BY DAYLIGHT, ABSENT WHENEVER THE WIND IS OFF THE WATER, AND IT IS STRONGER AT THE FOOT OF THE OUTSIDE STAIR THAN ON THE LANDING.** She is still in that room on the terms she was in that room on in February, nobody put her in it, nobody arranged one word of it, and nobody has been on that roof. She put the sash up because it has been warm, she looked at her cooker twice, she knows a matted gas pipe in the front of that room is not this, and on the sixteenth she took a plastic chair out on to the concrete at about twenty to five and slept on it and put it back at half past six. **The woman in the room next door along came out once at about twenty past five because her own window takes nothing, said it was the gas, was told she had it right, said right, and left her own door where it always is. HE TOOK THAT STAIR AT ABOUT TEN WITH A BAG, TOLD HER IT WAS THE RIVER COMING UP UNDER THERE WHEN THE WIND IS OFF THE WATER, SAID A VENT ON THE BACK OF THAT BLOCK WAS THE LAST THING HE WOULD BE LOOKING AT, AND DID NOT KNOW THE WOMAN NEXT DOOR ALONG HAD SAID IT WAS THE GAS, and has not been up that stair again, and neither woman in that building will ever be told which of them was nearer it.**
- **THE DAY'S MONEY WENT UNDER A COUNTER IN A SAFE AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR ON THE SEVENTEENTH OF AUGUST.** The woman who works in a shop counted the day's money on her own table in her own kitchen: notes first in the order they came out of the till, then coin, then the small silver rolled in her hand and counted twice. It came out within a small amount of what the other three Thursdays of that month came out to, which means nothing. **Nobody in that shop knows what the figure was and nobody in that borough has asked her to say.**
- **A STEP LADDER WENT FROM A BENCH IN A YARD OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD TO THE BACK ROOM OF A SHOP OFF A PARADE AND BACK AGAIN, AND IT IS IN THE BACK OF A VAN UNDER A BLANKET, AND A SPANNER IS IN ANOTHER MAN'S POCKET.** The man in his forties put it on that bench on the fifth of August and took it out of that back room on the sixteenth and never told anybody. The man in his thirties took it out of that yard on the sixth and put it on a counter and said one question and did not say where it was from, and took it out of that back room again on the twentieth of September. **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP SAW BOTH MEN DO ONE OF THE TWO THINGS EACH AND HAS ASKED NEITHER OF THEM ANYTHING, AND SHE DOES NOT KNOW WHERE THE LADDER CAME FROM.** Both men are certain neither of them believes the other ever touched it, neither is going to ask, and to put it to him would be to ask where it came from, which is the question neither can stand.
- **A HOUSE AT THE TOP OF A LANE WAS SAID IN A HALL ON A METER TO BE EMPTY AND IT IS NOT EMPTY, AND THE SENTENCE WENT ONE PLACE FURTHER AND CAME BACK WITH THE WRONG LANE ON IT, AND A MAN WHO WORKS NIGHTS HAS BEEN GOING A QUARTER OF A MILE FURTHER ON HIS WALK EACH MORNING FOR ABOUT FIVE WEEKS AND CANNOT GIVE ONE REASON FOR IT.** The woman who works in a shop repeated it on the twenty-third of August at a quarter to six, unasked, and put a lane a few hundred yard out of its place, and she was wrong by that much and not by anything else, and she does not know that she was wrong. The lane he used has seven houses and a family in the last of them with a car standing at the kerb and a porch light burning. **The man in his sixties still believes he passed on to this borough the news of a house standing empty, and it was not standing empty, there is no for sale board on that house and there never was one, and nobody in this borough will ever climb that lane and establish it.** No fifth of anything was produced, the third answer was not corrected, the fourth was not called a fifth, and nobody was told which day any collection comes on.
- **A WOMAN TRADING ON THAT PARADE PUT A BAR ACROSS HER OWN SHUTTER AT HALF PAST TEN ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF AUGUST WITH ABOUT NINE PEOPLE INSIDE HER AND THE LIGHT ON AND WENT UP AGAIN AT ONE, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO WAS TOLD ABOUT IT WAS A MAN GOING TO A BUS, WHO TOLD THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP ON THE THURSDAY.** It was not a closure and about thirty people went past and none of them said a word to anybody. **AND ON THE FRIDAY A MAN LOST A WHOLE DAY WAITING IN A HOUSE IN THAT STREET BECAUSE A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY WAS AT A CLINIC WITH HER MOTHER, AND HE SPENT SIX HOURS OF IT IN AN EMPTY CAR PARK READING A PAPER, AND HE TOLD THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP AND SHE PUT IT NOWHERE.** The only person given both of those is a third of a mile from both, and she has never spoken to the man who told her about the shutter and does not know his name.
- **A BIRD BOX IS LYING FACE DOWN ON A PATH AT THE FOOT OF A GABLE AT THE LOW END OF A STREET OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD, WITH THE TWO SCREWS THAT HELD IT STILL STANDING IN THE RENDER ABOVE, AND ABOUT FOUR FOOT OF POINTING DONE BESIDE IT THAT NOBODY ORDERED AND NOBODY HAS PAID FOR.** The box was taken off that wall on Tuesday the twenty-ninth of August by a man in his forties, who went there because two other men had told him somebody was going to have that wall done, which is not what was said. He did not put the box back and he is not going to. **The man in his thirties has said five times, in July and August and this month, that he would have a box made and put on to that gable before September is out, and he said it for the fifth time at half past nine on the thirtieth of August to a man who wanted nothing from him, and he did not do it.** The woman who works in a shop has the whole of it and says that path will still have it on it at the weekend.
- **A SEWING MACHINE ON A WOODEN STAND WAS TURNED DOWN IN ONE WORD AT THE TOP OF AN OUTSIDE STAIR ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF AUGUST, AND THE MAN WHO CARRIED IT UP NINE TREADS SAID NOTHING AT ALL AFTERWARDS AND HAS NOT BEEN UP THAT STAIR SINCE, AND A CHAIR WAS LEFT AGAINST A DIFFERENT DOOR IN A STREET OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD ON THE SECOND OF SEPTEMBER AND IS STILL IN A FRONT ROOM AT NUMBER FOURTEEN, WHERE IT DOES NOT MATCH AND IS A FOOT TOO WIDE AND ONE SPINDLE IS A DIFFERENT COLOUR.** The chair went out of the same house, and it went up that street because the van was already loaded and he had business at the next door along. **The man with a van told the woman who works in a shop about the sewing machine and said nothing about the chair, and the man in his forties worked at the next house all day and found the chair on a step and says nothing about it, and not one of the two women ever heard a word about the other.**
- **A BULB WENT OUT IN THE CEILING OF THAT KITCHEN AT THE BACK OF A PARADE ON THE TENTH OF SEPTEMBER, AND THE MAN WHO COOKS IN THAT KITCHEN HAS BEEN INTO SIX SHOPS SINCE THE MIDDLE OF JULY AND HAS NEVER ONCE SAID WHAT THE CEILING TAKES.** It is a bayonet cap and every shop in this borough sells the small screw cap. **One person in that room had known for about a week which bulb it takes, and he has never said the word either, and the bulb in his torch is a bayonet one and is the last he has.** He put the torch down on a low wall beside a bus stop at the top of a road at about half past four that afternoon and left no note. **The woman of about fifty-five stopped him in that corridor at about half past one and asked him where he was going to put it and he said he did not know yet, and she said right and used nothing he told her.** The torch is on that wall and it was still there on the Monday and still there on the Tuesday. The woman with a key was at that stop at about ten past five and looked at it about nine foot away and did not pick it up, and there is no version of a woman on a pavement with a torch in her hand and no reason to have it.
- **THE SASH WINDOW AT THE END OF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR IN A BLOCK AT THE TOP OF A STREET WAS PAINTED SHUT BEFORE THE FOUR DOORS ON THAT LANDING WERE PAINTED, AND THE THIN DRAB GREEN UNDER THE TOP COAT ON IT RUNS ACROSS THE SEAM WHERE THE TWO HALVES MEET, AND A WEDGE OF OFFCUT HAS BEEN UNDER IT SINCE THE LAST HOT WEEK AND IT HAS NOT MOVED.** The man in his forties found that out by putting his hand on the bottom rail while he was setting a bit into plaster and finding that it did not move at all. **THE DOORS WENT ON ABOUT TWELVE YEARS AGO, ALL FOUR OFF THE SAME TIN, AND THAT BUILDING STOPPED BEING ANYBODY'S RESPONSIBILITY WELL BEFORE THE SUMMER, AND THERE IS NO PAPER ABOUT THAT WINDOW ANYWHERE IN THIS BOROUGH, AND A STRANGER WITH A LADDER AND AN AFTERNOON COULD HAVE SHUT ONE LIKE THAT IN ANY DECADE OF THE LAST FIFTY, AND NOBODY IN THAT STREET IS GOING TO ADD ANYTHING TO THAT.** Behind that sash is number three, and the woman in number three has a chair pulled up to the glass and a radio going, and has not touched that sash since long before the hot weather and did not try to open it during the warm spell, and the wedge somebody else put there is the only thing in that passage that has moved in the best part of a year.
- **AND THE SHOPKEEPER ON THAT PARADE WRITES DOWN WHAT HER SHOP WANTS FOR THE WEEK, ON THE REVERSE OF A PAD LEAF, EACH SUNDAY NIGHT, AND ON TWO OF THOSE SHEETS THERE IS A TIME OF DAY WRITTEN FOUR LINES BELOW THE FRUIT IN THE SAME PENCIL IN THE SAME HAND, AND IT IS NOT IN LINE WITH ANYTHING.** The shop opens at eight and has never opened its door at that hour in its life. She wrote it twice, three days of shopping apart, on two sheets of the same size out of the same pad, and cannot account for it, and nothing in this borough would let her account for it, because a page like that holds no day beside any line on it. **The earlier of those two sheets is still in the pad.**
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FIVE SAID THE SAME SENTENCE TWICE, FOUR DAYS APART, AND IT CAME BACK AS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AND SHE CANNOT TELL WHICH SHE MEANT.** On Sunday the seventeenth, in the hall on a meter after the meeting broke up, to the man in his forties, who was at the far end with a cloth: **"Nobody has ever made a list of it."** He said nothing for four seconds and said no and went on with the cloth, and his pad stayed shut in the bag at his feet. On Tuesday the nineteenth, at a bus stop, to a man in a fleece going to a bus, the same sentence, and he told her that nothing in that building is on any list of anything, and got on the bus, and asked her nothing. **ON THE WEDNESDAY EVENING THE PAIR OF THEM STOOD IN THAT HALL WITHIN FOUR FEET OF EACH OTHER ON THE SAME SIDE OF THE ROOM FOR TWENTY MINUTES AND NEITHER MENTIONED THE SENTENCE, AND EACH WAS WITHOUT THE OTHER'S HALF OF IT, AND THE ONLY THING THEY SAID TO EACH OTHER WAS THAT THE TEA HAD GONE COLD AGAIN.**
- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A card has been in a slot nine hundred and forty-seven days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post nine hundred and forty-nine days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 345–354 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The counter in the vestibule is in no scene in this batch, answered nothing and was not entered, and **the drawer under it is not opened, not counted, not asked about and not mentioned, and the word *drawer* is at zero in all ten chapters, as is the word *envelope*.** The rota is not rebuilt and the 11:00–13:30 row is not mentioned. The second line under Bram Osei's Thursday has not been looked at. The board is at seven of nine. The ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and untold. The buff file of 14 October 2011 is not opened and four people have seen the bay number and the date on that cover. Paragraph 4.11 is not answered and the compliance week is not verified. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines included, the word *spring* is at zero, the word *forty* is at zero, the stem *drain* is at zero, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven.** The order is not in force and was not brought back to life.

- **AND THE SIX OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE WAS ANSWERED. From 0184, which dated thing will be the first somebody can prove — **still open, and it went the other way again for the fifth batch in a row: a house at the top of a lane was said in a hall on a meter to be empty and the sentence went one place further and came back with the wrong lane on it, and a man who works nights has been going a quarter of a mile further on his walk each morning for about five weeks over it, and nobody has corrected the woman who repeated it and nobody is going to. No fifth of anything has been produced, the third answer has not been corrected, the fourth has not been called a fifth, and nobody has been told which day any collection comes on. From 0174, which of the eight will be the first somebody decides to keep — **untouched. From 0164, whether the page or the chair is the first thing a person is asked to agree to — **a shopping list carries a time of day that is not what the list is for and nobody was asked; a man carried a pad in his bag through a whole afternoon and said the fifth thing he had ever said to do with the pad in his hand and did not write on it; a woman counted a figure on her own kitchen table and wrote it on the back of her own hand and put it in her own safe; and a man in his forties read a page of a pad in a van in August and a man in his thirties had a pad in his hand all afternoon in September and did not open it on the street where he said a thing for the fifth time. From 0192, which of two opposite shapes this city has to stop, and what a person has to be holding to stop each — **still open, and the second of them is still nothing. The volume's own, about a permission, an obligation and a right of return — **untouched, and the word *permission* is at zero in all ten chapters. And the sixth, about a document and not a person — **no piece of paper that leaves a building was created in this batch; the things that came were a smell, a chair, a spanner, a bird box, two screws, a sewing machine, a wedge of wood, nine coats of paint, a drab green undercoat, a pad, a shopping list, a day's money and a carrier bag of lunch things, and nobody has a copy of any of it.**

- **AND FIVE FLAGS GO TO A HUMAN AND NONE OF THEM IS A WRITER'S. THE NAME OF THE CITY, WHICH THE PROSE CALLS TIDEGLASS, AND *NACRE* AND *RIVER STACKS* AND EVERY DISTRICT NAME APPEAR NOWHERE IN CHAPTERS 345–354. WHAT VOLUME 05 IS CENTRALLY, WITH THE OPERATIVE RULES BINDING AND NO ROOT TERM EXPIRED AND NO RECLAMATION SEASON OPENED AND THE WORD *RECLAMATION* IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND A MONTH NOT BEING A SEASON.
