## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 364 — Tuesday the thirty-first of October 2017, from about half past seven in the morning until about eight in the evening, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A FEW SMALL REPAIRS NOBODY HAS SPOKEN ABOUT, ONE OBJECT IN TWO DISPUTED KEEPERS, ONE RUMOUR WITH NOTHING INSIDE IT, ONE ACT NOBODY WILL ADMIT TO, TWO COUNTS OF THE SAME HEAP THAT BOTH STAND, ONE SAMPLE OF A TRADE LEFT WHERE ONE MAN MIGHT SEE IT, ONE ENTRY IN ONE BOOK WITH THE WRONG HOUR IN IT, ONE CHAIR THAT CHANGED WALLS, AND A LINE UNDER THE LAST ENTRY OF ANOTHER BOOK THAT IS STILL EMPTY. A FORTNIGHT WENT THROUGH AND NONE OF IT WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES CARRIES FOUR THINGS HE CANNOT SAY AND HAS SAID TO NOBODY.** That he made a thing on brackets in a room in June. That he put a door back the right way on his own time and told nobody. That he stood in a dark passage and found a door behaving in a way he could not account for, and has not been back to that end of it. And that he is the third person in this borough to work out how long the empty line under the last entry of a register has been waiting, and that he did it tonight standing up, with the bay door open in front of him, and went home. **NONE OF THE FOUR WAS EVER OFFERED TO ANYBODY AND NONE OF THEM WILL BE.**
- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP CARRIES A RACK SHE PUT RIGHT HERSELF, A SENTENCE SHE STOPPED IN THE MIDDLE OF, AND TWO FIGURES SHE WAS GIVEN AND PUT NOWHERE.** She has been carrying the first since August and has mentioned it to nobody, including herself. She has spent two days wishing she could put the rest of a second thing back. **AND SHE STANDS BEHIND THAT COUNTER AS SHE DID A MONTH AGO AND NOTHING THAT HAPPENED IN THAT SHOP IN THIS FORTNIGHT EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT.**
- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES CARRIES A HAMMER THAT IS NOT HIS AND A RACK THAT IS SQUARE AND A COUNT THAT IS ON A SLATE UNDER ANOTHER MAN'S, AND HE KNOWS NONE OF THE THREE.** A good tool is on a shelf over his own bench with the maker's name taped over, and he has a nail in his thumb from almost undoing the tape. **HE HAS PUT IT THERE AND HE HAS NOT SAID WHERE IT CAME FROM, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO, AND HE HAS ALSO NEVER ASKED WHY A THING HE WAS TOLD ABOUT IN AUGUST NO LONGER NEEDS ATTENDING TO.** He also walks through an archway twice a day past something on a stone shelf that he has never looked at, and there is a man across the road from that archway who knows exactly what is on that shelf and has watched him go past twice.
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FIVE CARRIES A HANDRAIL SHE HAS SAID NOTHING ABOUT, WHICH IS NOTHING, BECAUSE TO HER A HANDRAIL IS A HANDRAIL.** She has handled a hammer she does not believe is hers and a stair rail she believes is perfectly ordinary, and she is a fortnight older than she was and no further along in anything, and she is still in that room on the same terms, and nobody put her there.
- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES CARRIES A SENTENCE IN A BOOK HE HAS NEVER READ.** He put a chain on a door he had no business touching and he has a fair idea that what he did is written down somewhere, and he has not asked and is not going to, because a man who asks is a man who is asking to be found out. **HE HAS ALSO SPENT ABOUT FIFTY MINUTES IN A HALL THIS FORTNIGHT WITH A CUP, UNDER A CHAIR THAT WAS ON THE WRONG SIDE OF THE ROOM, WITHOUT ONCE REGISTERING WHICH SIDE IT WAS ON.**
- **THE MAN WHO WORKS NIGHTS CARRIES SOMETHING HE GOT OFF A MAN HE DOES NOT KNOW AND PASSED ON IN THREE WORDS, AND THE PASSING-ON IS THE ONLY PART THAT WAS HIS.**
- **THE MAN AT THE END OF A LANDING, WHO HAS NO AGE ANYWHERE IN THIS ACCOUNT AND MAY NOT BE GIVEN ONE, CARRIES FOUR SCREWS AND A LANDING WITH NO LIGHT ON IT AND A QUESTION HE DID NOT ASK.** A woman of about fifty-five told him his own handrail was sound going past his own door and he asked her a question about somebody's mother instead. **HE HAS BEGUN, WITHOUT DECIDING TO, A SUM ABOUT HOW LONG AGO A THING IS, AND IT HAS NO ZERO IN IT, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO PUT ANY OF IT DOWN IN FRONT OF ANYBODY.**
- **AND ON THE WALL OF A YARD BEHIND TWO HOUSES THERE IS A SLATE WITH TWO FIGURES ON IT IN TWO HANDS, ONE ABOVE THE OTHER, AND NEITHER RUBBED OUT, AND ONE QUARTER OF WHAT IS IN THAT YARD IS WEARING A NUMBER THAT BELONGS TO SOME OTHER LOAD.** The two men who wrote them have both been told about the other one and neither will put his down, and the yard has no book and no preference, and a quarter of the answer is not a fraction of it, it is a different question.
- **AND ON A SHELF OF STONE IN A COVERED WAY AT THE TOP OF A STREET THERE IS ONE TILE WITH RAIN ON IT.** Nobody in that street knows it is there. One man knows exactly what it is and where it came off and is not going to be asked why he put it there.
- **AND ON AN UNLOCKED SHELF BEHIND A DOOR IN A HALL AT THE TOP OF A STREET THERE IS A BOOK WITH A DAY AND A SENTENCE IN IT, AND THE SENTENCE SAYS A DOOR WAS FOUND OPEN AT NINE O'CLOCK, AND NINE O'CLOCK IS THE HOUR THE MAN WHO KEEPS THAT BOOK DOES HIS ROUND AND NOT THE HOUR IT HAPPENED.** Nobody has ever asked for that book and nobody is going to. The one man who was in the building that night and could put a different hour on it did not look at one, there is not one on the route, and he does not know the book exists.
- **AND A CHAIR WITH A SPLIT IN THE BACK OF IT IS ON THE OTHER SIDE OF THE HALL ON CARROW LANE FROM WHERE IT WAS FOURTEEN DAYS AGO, AND THE MAN WHO HANGS THOSE CHAIRS UP HAS NEVER HAD TO THINK ABOUT A CHAIR.** Nobody was watching a chair on a Wednesday, there is no book in that hall, nothing is drawn on its walls, and the one person in that room who might have dated it was on the other side of the floor with a cup.
- **AND IN A BAY AT THE END OF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR IN A BLOCK BY THE WATER, ON A DOOR THAT DOES NOT SHUT OVER ITS CATCH, A BOOK LIES OPEN AT ITS NINETEENTH AND LAST ENTRY WITH NOTHING UNDER IT AND NOTHING UNDER THAT.** It was not opened on any day of this fortnight. The lamp went in about two years back, under a heading somebody held back for that class of loss, and the paper carries on past it a couple of fingers and then stops well short of the foot of that volume, and nobody ever closed the gap and the book still has it.
- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE FORTNIGHT IS A FORTNIGHT AND IS NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** shutters up with the longest of them left to the end of the morning · a number said at a counter about fifteen times now and not answered, unmoved since the twelfth of January, and neither of the two people who say it could name two of what it counts · about nine people carrying nothing at all, and every one of them carrying something the other eight are not · a bus that was not the one a man was waiting for, three days running · an archway crossed six times and stopped in none of them.
- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A card has been in a slot nine hundred and eighty-four days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post nine hundred and eighty-six days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 355–364 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The counter in the vestibule is in no scene in this batch, answered nothing and was not entered, and **the drawer under it is not opened, not counted, not asked about and not mentioned, and the word *drawer* is at zero in all ten chapters, as is the word *envelope*.** The rota is not rebuilt and the 11:00–13:30 row is not mentioned. The second line under Bram Osei's Thursday has not been looked at. The board is at seven of nine. The ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and untold. The buff file of 14 October 2011 is not opened and four people have seen the bay number and the date on that cover. Paragraph 4.11 is not answered and the compliance week is not verified. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines included, the word *spring* is at zero, the word *forty* is at zero, the stem *drain* is at zero, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven.** The order is not in force and was not brought back to life.
- **AND THE SIX OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE WAS ANSWERED. From 0184, which dated thing will be the first somebody can prove — **still open, and it went the same way for the sixth batch in a row: a number said at a counter and not answered, a report that reached a man with a place in it and no content, and a chair that changed sides in a hall with no datable cause. No fifth of anything has been produced, the third answer has not been corrected, the fourth has not been called a fifth, and nobody has been told which day any collection comes on. From 0174, which of the eight will be the first somebody decides to keep — **untouched. From 0164, whether the page or the chair is the first thing a person is asked to agree to — **a chair in a hall is on the wrong side of the room and nobody was asked about it and nobody was asked why it is there; a man came through an archway with a bag of about a hundred and sixty offcuts and twelve of them were still in it on the Friday night; a man had a book in front of him on a table in a hall nine or ten times without taking any of it in; and a man stood in a market for twenty minutes and bought nothing. From 0192, which of two opposite shapes this city has to stop, and what a person has to be holding to stop each — **still open, and the second of them is still nothing. The volume's own, about a permission, an obligation and a right of return — **untouched, and the word *permission* is at zero in all ten chapters. And the sixth, about a document and not a person — **no piece of paper that leaves a building was created in this batch; the things that came were two wedges, four screws, a hammer, a tile, a figure chalked on a slate, a figure chalked under it and a line in a book on a shelf, and nobody has a copy of any of it.
- **AND FIVE FLAGS GO TO A HUMAN AND NONE OF THEM IS A WRITER'S. THE NAME OF THE CITY, WHICH THE PROSE CALLS TIDEGLASS, AND *NACRE* AND *RIVER STACKS* AND EVERY DISTRICT NAME APPEAR NOWHERE IN CHAPTERS 355–364. WHAT VOLUME 05 IS CENTRALLY, WITH THE OPERATIVE RULES BINDING AND NO ROOT TERM EXPIRED AND NO RECLAMATION SEASON OPENED AND THE WORD *RECLAMATION* IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND A FORTNIGHT NOT BEING A SEASON. THE BOUNDARY, WHICH IS A HUMAN'S: `outline/series.md` GIVES VOLUME 04 AS 145–192, VOLUME 05 AS 193–240, VOLUME 06 AS 241–288 AND VOLUME 07 AS 289–336, THE PAGE HAS 195 THROUGH 364 IN THE VOLUME 05 DIRECTORY, THE TWO DISAGREE AT BOTH ENDS, THERE IS NO FIGURE FOR THE END OF VOLUME 05 IN EITHER, THE FIGURE 242 IS USED BY NOBODY, NO CHAPTER IS MOVED BETWEEN DIRECTORIES, NO OUTLINE IS REWRITTEN, NO VOLUME 06 OUTLINE AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT EXISTS, AND CARD 0344, CARD 0354 AND CARD 0364 ARE ALL NOT CLOSES. THE SPELLING OF THE THIRD OF THE EIGHT PLACES, WHICH WAS NOT TOUCHED IN THIS BATCH BECAUSE THE EIGHT PLACES ARE NOT READ OUT IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, SO NEITHER SPELLING APPEARS AND THE FLAG CARRIES FORWARD UNDECIDED; **THE CENSUS OF THE STATE FILES THAT PRINT SPELLINGS IS CARRIED AT §11 OF THE `state/open-threads.md` BLOCK AND IS NOT REPEATED HERE AND NO SPELLING IS NAMED IN ANY FILE.**** AND A FIFTH, CARRIED FROM CONTINUATION 0015: **THE DATE ARITHMETIC IN THE PROMPTS HAS BEEN WRONG IN ONE OF ITS TWO READINGS IN FIVE PROMPTS IN A ROW, A DATE WAS CHOSEN THAT SATISFIES BOTH READINGS AT ONCE, AND NO CHAPTER WAS MOVED TO MEET EITHER.**

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 374 — Thursday the seventh of December 2017, from about half past seven in the morning until about half past eight in the evening, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A SMALL JOB THAT TOOK THREE TIMES WHAT WAS QUOTED AND A HOUSEHOLD THAT SAID NOTHING, A SET OF THINGS FOR A FIRE IN A ROOM WITH NO FIRE, A SENTENCE HEARD TWO WAYS BY TWO MEN, A SURFACE NOBODY CAN EXPLAIN AND A WET STEP UNDER ONE DOOR, AN ERRAND THAT FAILED ON ONE MORNING, A BARROW AND A QUESTION IN A GAP, A FIGURE A MAN HAS BEEN WRONG ABOUT FOR NINE YEARS, A PIECE OF STONE ON A LANDING, A MONDAY ON WHICH NOTHING HAPPENED, AND A SMELL NOBODY CAN DATE. A FORTNIGHT WENT THROUGH AND NONE OF IT WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES CARRIES SEVEN THINGS HE CANNOT SAY AND HAS SAID TO NOBODY.** That a small job took four days where a day and a half was quoted, and that a man half a mile off stood waiting for him for two hours and then began. That a surface was laid along a block of flats over twelve weekends, and that a woman with a wet step has never been told by him who did it, and that he could have said a name at the fourth minute of a conversation and did not and is never going to. That a sack of ice was not brought on one Friday and three people stood at a counter for ten seconds and none of them gave him anything to say it with. That a line under the last entry of a book has been blank for one thousand one hundred and seven days, and that on the seventh of December he stood in a corridor at the end of a warm stair with an open door and a recess in front of him and did not go in. That a woman has been wrong about a sack of sand for nine years and he is the one who is wrong and he is not going to be told and has not asked. And that a sweet smell has been coming into that parade under an awning every morning for as long as anybody in it can remember, and that no date on it is available to him whatever, because he was there every morning before it and every morning after it and there is nothing in him that sorts one of those mornings from another. **NONE OF THE SEVEN WAS EVER OFFERED TO ANYBODY AND NONE OF THEM WILL BE.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES CARRIES A SURFACE, A BARROW HE DID NOT GET, A QUESTION HE DID NOT ASK, AND A SIGHTING HE WILL NOT REPORT, AND HE KNOWS NONE OF THEM AS A THING HE IS CARRYING.** He laid a hard surface along a block of flats out of his own wages, in his own time, on twelve Saturdays, and told nobody he was starting, and nobody stopped him, and nobody has ever asked him what it was for. He wanted a barrow that stood at the back of that shop for twelve days, and he came into that shop four times in that time, stopped at it twice and said nothing, and on two other mornings went round the back of that parade rather than go in, and walked past the other man securing it to a pickup without turning round, and said no at a rack when the question was put to him in a gap. He came into the back of a shop one afternoon with a question of his own and did not ask it, and has asked himself twice since how many people come into that hall. And he stood on the off side of a street for no reason of his own and saw a man take a short flight of steps in two halves, and has not told anybody and it is not in him to. **HE IS ON NOTHING AND NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP CARRIES NINE WORDS SHE SAID ONCE AND WILL NEVER LEARN WHAT TWO MEN TOOK OUT OF THEM, AN ERRAND THAT DID NOT HAPPEN, A QUESTION SHE PUT IN A GAP, AND A SMELL SHE CAN DATE TO THE DAY.** She closed a subject with nine words in front of four people and two of them heard something else in it, and she has not asked either of them and is not going to, because a woman who asks what two men heard has admitted there was more than one of it. She shut a chest that had not been filled, put a cloth on the outside of it, and told nobody, and let a man go out of her shop at ten past six without a word when he was the one who was late. She wrote a price on a card for a barrow she did not want, and said fourteen days later in a gap at a rack whether he had wanted it, and does not know he had paused there. **AND SHE IS ONE OF TWO PEOPLE IN THAT BOROUGH WHO CAN PUT A DATE NEAR A SWEET SMELL IN A PARADE, AND THE OTHER ONE IS A MAN WHO CAME ALONG FOUR DAYS LATER, AND NEITHER OF THEM CAN SAY WHEN IT ARRIVED.** Nothing that happened to her in this fortnight exists anywhere outside it.

- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FIVE CARRIES A THING IN HER ROOM SHE CANNOT ACCOUNT FOR AND A FIGURE SHE IS NOT GOING TO TELL ANYBODY.** No fire has ever been lit in that room by anybody, and the arrangement for one is not in the room at all, and a set of implements for it is standing on her floor and she will not move it because moving it is an admission, and she has hung a jug on the rail of it for want of anywhere else in a small room to hang one. **SHE WORKED OUT, STANDING IN AN OPEN GATE WITH TWO JUGS IN HER HANDS, THAT A MAN HAS BEEN WRONG ABOUT THE WEIGHT OF A SACK OF SAND FOR NINE YEARS, AND SHE SAID NOTHING, AND SHE IS NOT GOING TO, AND IT IS NOTHING TO HER BESIDES.** She went up that outside stair twice more that week with a jug in each hand and did not go along that corridor.

- **THE MAN AT THE END OF A LANDING, WHO HAS NO AGE ANYWHERE IN THIS ACCOUNT AND MAY NOT BE GIVEN ONE, CARRIES FOUR SCREWS, A LANDING WITH NO LIGHT ON IT, A QUESTION HE DID NOT ASK, A SET OF FIRE THINGS IN A ROOM HE PUT THEM IN AND SAID NOTHING ABOUT, AND A PIECE OF STONE HE CARRIED BACK DOWN ONE FLIGHT AND LEFT AGAINST A WALL.** He put the set down in the middle of her floor at ten at night because he had nowhere to put it and went out into the yard. **HE DOES NOT KNOW THAT THE WOMAN IN THE ROOM NEXT DOOR ALONG HAS SEEN IT, OR THAT THE WOMAN WHOSE ROOM IT IS IN HAS SEEN IT, OR THAT EITHER OF THEM HAS SAID ANYTHING TO ANYBODY.** He is also the only man in that house who has ever had a hand on the rail and the only one who knows and the only one who cannot say.

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES CARRIES NOTHING, WHICH IS THE POINT OF HIM TWICE OVER.** Six years of one afternoon a week in a hall at the back, and not on anything in any of it, and in November a woman said nine words across a table and he took them as being about himself, and nothing had been going on round him and nothing is shut, and he is not going to ask. In December he sat on a low wall beside a yard gate for twenty minutes with a cup and took in, from thirty yard, a man stopping in a road and looking upward, went home before midday, and could not have described the man to anybody afterwards. **HE SAID NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER IN EITHER CHAPTER AND NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER WAS SAID IN FRONT OF HIM IN EITHER.**

- **AND ON THE GROUND ALONG ONE SIDE OF A BLOCK AT THE TOP OF A STREET THERE IS A HARD SURFACE OF ABOUT A HUNDRED AND THIRTY SQUARE YARD THAT WAS LAID BETWEEN AUGUST AND NOVEMBER BY A MAN NOBODY ASKED, AND SIX OF THE EIGHT PEOPLE IN THAT BUILDING HAVE DESCRIBED IT AND EVERY DESCRIPTION IS A DIFFERENT ONE.** Four of the six are for something or other: a way across to the gate, somewhere for the bins, somewhere to stand a bicycle, a waste of good stone put down too late in the year. One is about mud coming in off the lane and is the only one a person could put a hand on. The sixth is that nobody can say whether even that one is true. **THE MAN WHO LAID IT HAS BEEN ASKED ONE QUESTION ABOUT IT BY ANYBODY IN THAT BUILDING IN THREE MONTHS AND THE QUESTION WAS WHETHER THE MIX WAS STRONG ENOUGH.**

- **AND IN A PASSAGE AT THE BACK OF A SHOP OFF A PARADE THERE IS A SACK OF ICE ON ITS SIDE AGAINST A STACK OF MILK CRATES.** It came back with a second sack on a Saturday morning in November because a load had come in on the Thursday, and the shopkeeper took the other one and has not mentioned this one since, and the man who brought it has not been in that shop to say anything about it.

- **AND ON THE HALF-LANDING OF A STAIR IN A BLOCK OF FLATS, OPPOSITE A MAN'S OWN FRONT DOOR, THERE IS A PIECE OF AN OLD HEARTH, A FOOT AND A HALF SQUARE AND FOUR INCH THICK, AGAINST A WALL, AND HE COULD HAVE CARRIED IT INSIDE AND DID NOT.** It got there because a man who was going out with his bag took the other end of it when two men could not turn it, and they went down the stair and did not come back, and he cannot ask who it came from and cannot give it back, and it is still there, and a woman who works in a shop goes past it sideways twice a day and has never mentioned it.

- **AND UNDER A TARPALINE ALONG A BRICK WALL IN A YARD BEHIND A HOUSE OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD THERE ARE NINE SACKS OF PLASTER AND BOARDING AND OLD INSULATION.** The woman of that house offered him the back of that yard for them in November and he told her there was no need to hurry, and the subject has not come up between them since.

- **AND THERE HAS BEEN A SWEET SMELL UNDER AN AWNING IN THE MIDDLE OF THAT PARADE EVERY MORNING FOR AS LONG AS ANYBODY IN IT CAN REMEMBER, AND IT IS STILL THERE ON THE SEVENTH OF DECEMBER, AND NOBODY CAN PUT A DATE ON IT.** Nobody knows what it is. The woman who runs that shop has not said who lives in the flat above it. The man who does the hall says it is a pipe on that side. The man in his forties says it is the weather, which it is not. **A WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP CAN DATE IT TO THE DAY AND THE MAN WHO WALKS THAT PARADE SEVERAL TIMES A DAY CANNOT DATE IT AT ALL, AND THE REASON IS THAT HE WAS THERE BEFORE IT.**

- **AND IN A RECESS OFF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR IN A BLOCK BY THE WATER, ON A DOOR THAT DOES NOT SHUT OVER ITS CATCH, A BOOK LIES ON A SHELF WITH ITS BACK TO THE INSIDE FACE OF THAT DOOR.** It is open at its nineteenth and last entry with nothing under it and nothing under that, and the last thing entered against any heading concerns a lamp outside a yard, entered a couple of Novembers back as lost against a heading somebody held open for that sort of loss, and not one line has been entered under that heading since, and the paper on that page carries on past that entry by about two fingers and then gives out a long way short of the foot of the volume, and the gap has never been tidied by anybody. **A LINE UNDER THAT LAST ENTRY HAS BEEN BLANK FOR ONE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED AND SEVEN DAYS. IT WAS NOT TOUCHED IN THIS FORTNIGHT. NOBODY WENT INTO THAT RECESS AND NOBODY COUNTED ANYTHING ON THAT STAIR.**

- **AND A BUILDER'S BARROW IS IN A YARD OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD BEING USED TO MOVE ABOUT NINE TONS OF STUFF IN A WEEK.** A man in his thirties wanted it for the twelve days it stood there and a man in his forties bought it in front of him, and neither of them has raised it, and they were in the same yard for two hours thirteen days later.

- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE FORTNIGHT IS A FORTNIGHT AND IT IS NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** shutters up in an unaltered order with the longest left to the end of the morning · a coil of wire at about half past ten and a bag of plugs at about ten past four · a man off nights in that parade at about half past four · a man in his sixties out on a low wall with a cup for about an hour · a hall on a meter open on the Sunday from ten until four with the urn going and about fourteen people through it and the man behind the counter asked nothing by anybody · a man who lost two hours of a Monday's work and went home at about half past seven · nine people through a fortnight with nothing on them, every one of them holding what the other eight were not holding, and not one afternoon in which one of them said anything to another about his own.

- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A card has been in a slot one thousand and twenty-one days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post one thousand and twenty-three days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 365–374 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. ## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 384 — Saturday the twentieth of January 2018, from about half past seven in the morning until about half past nine at night, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A PIECE OF WOOD CUT FOR SOMEBODY WHO DID NOT ASK FOR IT, A CHEST IN A MAN'S FRONT ROOM WITH HALF A WORD ON THE LID, A QUESTION ABOUT AN HOUR OF THE MORNING THAT TWO MEN WANT AND NEITHER WILL ASK, A LANDING THAT GOES DARK EARLY AND THREE PEOPLE WHO EACH HAVE AN ACCOUNT OF IT, ONE NO SAID AT A KERB, A VAN AND A MACHINE AN HOUR AND A BIT APART, A MARK ON A LANDING WALL THAT TWO PEOPLE LOOKED AT ON THE SAME MORNING, FIVE WORDS ON A STAIR, A WALL THAT IS BETTER THAN THE MAN WHO WARNED PEOPLE OFF IT SAYS IT IS, AND A FORTNIGHT IN WHICH NOTHING AT ALL WAS DIFFERENT. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES CARRIES FOUR THINGS HE CANNOT SAY AND HAS SAID TO NOBODY.** That a piece of a worktop was rotten in a house he lives in and a man he had never met cut a new edge of it and neither of them will ever mention it. That a tea chest was put into a stranger's arms on a footway and he is the only person in this borough who has it and he is not going to be the one to ask. That a landing light came on early four times and a man with the keys to that block walked up that stair four days later with his hand on the wall and looked up at the fitting and went on, and nobody said a word. And that a line under the last entry of a book in a bay off a corridor in that block by the water is at one thousand one hundred and fifty-one days, and that he has now stopped in that corridor three times and has not gone in any of them and does not know there is a book in there. **NONE OF THE FOUR WAS EVER OFFERED TO ANYBODY AND NONE OF THEM WILL BE.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES CARRIES FIVE THINGS AND KNOWS NONE OF THEM AS A THING HE IS CARRYING.** A hard surface he laid along a block of flats in another batch, a barrow he did not get, a question he did not ask, a man he watched taking some steps in two halves. To that he has added: a chest that arrived in his arms in a street in the middle of December and a stencil on its lid he has not tried to read; a freezer he has not offered to a second person since one word was said at the kerb; a man with a bin at a kerb who watched a van for a minute and a bit and never said so; two people on a landing looking at the same mark; and five words said past him in a passage that he took as being about a man on the floor above him, which is exactly what they were about. **HE IS ON NOTHING AND NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP CARRIES ONE ANSWER SHE GAVE TWICE AND ONE SHE DID NOT TURN ROUND FOR.** On a Monday in the middle of that batch she said, to a man with a van and not to anybody else, that she was in from half seven, that the door was shut until nine, and that she was not going to be walking through her own shop in a coat before she had her sleeves up. Two men in that room heard it and neither of them heard the hour in it. She has not asked either of them what they took. In December she had her back to the window of her own shop while a man set a freezer down in a pickup at the kerb and lifted it back in again, and she kept her back to that window until the pickup had gone up the street, and she turned round afterwards and there was nothing out there to look at, and she has not asked, and she has not found a question she could put to a man she knows nothing about without admitting she wanted the answer. **NOTHING THAT HAPPENED TO HER IN THIS BATCH EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT.**

- **THE MAN WHO WORKS NIGHTS CARRIES A BALANCE IN A BUILDING BY THE WATER AND A QUESTION HE IS NOT GOING TO PUT TO ANYBODY.** On the morning of the sixth of January he was up at about ten past ten, having not been able to get back to sleep since two, and came out of his own front door for a paper, and there was a pale rectangle on the paper of that landing where a chest had stood for six days, and the woman who lives on that landing was standing at the far end of it with her back to him, looking at the same mark. He went back in and shut it. He has not put it to her and does not mean to, and he has no idea she was on that landing at all. **HE IS ONE OF NINE PEOPLE IN THIS BOROUGH WHO ARE ON NOTHING, AND HE IS NOT ON ANYTHING, AND HIS NOT BEING ASKED IS NOT SOMEBODY'S BUSINESS.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES CARRIES FIVE WORDS HE THOUGHT HE HAD GOT ACROSS AND A HALF-LANDING HE DECIDED HE HAD ALREADY SEEN.** On a Wednesday morning in January he said five words in a passage to nobody while a man went past him, and went home that day believing the man had taken them in. He was never told otherwise and is not going to be. In December he stood on a landing in another block and worked out for himself that somebody had been holding a switch down, and he has not mentioned that either, and he is wrong about it, and nobody will put him right. **HE SAID NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER WAS SAID IN FRONT OF HIM IN ANY OF THEM.**

- **AND ON THE BACK WALL OF A YARD BY A RAILWAY ARCH THERE IS A WALL THAT IS A GOOD DEAL BETTER THAN THE MAN WHO HAS TRADED OUT OF THAT YARD FOR DECADES SAYS IT IS, AND THE YOUNGER MAN IN THAT YARD KNOWS, AND IS CARRYING ON AS HE WAS.** He found it on his knees with a claw hammer, on a batten he had fixed the hard way in the autumn that would not come off because the resin had gone right round the screw and set. Nothing he has built on that warning since the summer is out of place and nothing in this borough is coming back in a year over a plug. **THE OLDER MAN WILL SAY THE SAME THING NEXT MONTH AND EVERY MONTH AFTER IT, AND NOBODY HAS PUT IT TO HIM AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.**

- **AND IN A YARD AT THE TOP OF THAT TOWN HE HAS THE USE OF AND NOT THE RUNNING OF, UNDER A SHEET OF ROOFING FELT, THERE IS A CHEST THAT IS NOT ANYBODY'S IN THIS BOROUGH.** Two men set it down outside a flat on the last Sunday of that year because it was in the way of the floor they were there to lift. It stood there six days. It went out on a Friday with nobody asked and it has not been missed by anybody who knew it was there, because nobody knew it was there. The man who took it away was in that building ten minutes and knocked on none of the four doors and is not going to. **HIS OWN YARD AT THE TOP OF THAT TOWN IS ALSO WHERE A FREEZER HE BOUGHT IN OCTOBER IS, BEHIND A LOW WALL UNDER A PIECE OF HARDBOARD, AND HE HAS NOT PUT IT TO A SECOND PERSON SINCE HE WAS REFUSED IN ONE WORD AT A KERB.**

- **AND UNDER AN AWNING IN THE MIDDLE OF THAT PARADE THERE IS A SMELL IN THE MORNINGS, GONE BY TEN, AND IT IS STILL THERE, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO WALKED THAT STREET EVERY MORNING BEFORE IT ARRIVED STILL CANNOT PUT ANY DATE ON IT.** The woman who runs the shop that shuts early still has not said who lives above her. Nothing in that parade has turned anything over.

- **AND IN AN ALCOVE OFF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR IN A BLOCK BY THE WATER, BEHIND A DOOR THAT HAS NEVER CAUGHT, A BOOK STANDS OPEN ON A SHELF.** Nobody has added a twentieth line to it since that volume was last put back. Whoever wrote the nineteenth wrote it, wrote what he had been told about a lamp standing outside a yard, and shut the covers to, and the heading he put that under is one somebody left open for whatever else of that sort turns up, and nothing has been put under it since, and the page would take a good many more lines before it was full. **NOBODY WENT IN, NOBODY OPENED IT, NOBODY WROTE ANYTHING DOWN AND NOBODY COUNTED ANYTHING, AND THE ONLY MAN IN THAT BOROUGH WHO COULD HAVE PUT A FIGURE ON THAT LAST LINE BY COUNTING DID NOT COUNT IT.** There is nobody in that borough who could tell you what the rule under that last line is for, and there was nobody a fortnight ago either, and there will be nobody in a fortnight.

- **AND FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ARE STILL GOING ROUND, AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** They were given at one counter in December by three people who each had a point. On a Wednesday in this batch one of the men who gave one said in a shop that it was still not settled, and the other said no, and neither said which of the four he holds, and the woman at the till said nothing. No fifth of anything has been produced, the third answer has not been corrected, and the fourth has not been called a fifth.

- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE FORTNIGHT IS A FORTNIGHT AND IT IS NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** shutters up in an unaltered order with the last one at the top left to the end of the morning · a counter from ten to nine with the order on for the Friday · a coil of wire on a counter at ten past four and a bag of plugs at the same hour two days later · a man off nights up that parade at seven in the morning and into it again in the afternoon · a man in his sixties on a low wall with a cup for about an hour · a hall on a meter on the Saturday morning with the urn on and about sixteen people through it and a man behind the counter that nobody put anything to · a man who did an hour's work on a Thursday, came back down a warm stair, and stopped in a corridor · nine people through that fortnight carrying nothing for anybody else, and not one afternoon on which any of the nine reached out to another of the nine about anything at all

- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A card has been in a slot one thousand and sixty-five days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. ## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 394 — Saturday the third of March 2018, from about half past seven in the morning until about half past nine at night, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A TIN OF GREASE THAT IS NOT ON A BENCH, A CRATE OF APPLES THAT WENT OUT OF A GATE, A BELL THAT HAS NOT RUNG SINCE THE MIDDLE OF DECEMBER, A CHURN ON A LEDGE IN A SHARED PASSAGE, A FLOOR LAID IN A ROOM THAT IS NOT A FLAT, TWO RAILS THIRTEEN INCH APART, A CHAIR THAT NO LONGER WOBBLES, A CORNER OF A CEILING WITH TWO FOOT OF LATH OPEN IN IT, A TIN THAT WENT INTO A HAND AND BACK ONTO A LEDGE, A BOX ON A TABLE AT THE BACK OF A SHOP, AND FIFTY-FIVE MINUTES AT A GATE. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES CARRIES ELEVEN THINGS AND HAS TOLD NOBODY ABOUT ANY OF THEM.** A tin of grease he left in a yard and did not take, and a crate of apples he took out of that gate without asking. A bell he has never had done, said to a woman entitled to ask, and he does not know she asked. Thirty square foot of floor in a room he has no business in, with a woman looking in. A rail he cannot see a second of. A chair he put back better than he found it. A minute and a half about a ceiling that settled nothing. A tin in a hand in a street. A box he gave away by leaving it on a table. Fifty-five minutes at a gate on a Saturday morning. **NONE OF THE ELEVEN WAS OFFERED TO ANYBODY AND NONE OF THEM WILL BE, AND HE HAS NOT MENTIONED ONE OF THEM TO ANYBODY SINCE.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES CARRIES THINGS HE DOES NOT KNOW ARE A THING HE IS CARRYING.** A churn in his way in a passage he did not know was blocked, and six trips round it since. A second rail he does not know is in that building. A box on a table that is his. A hall with a table and a cup on it. **HE IS ON NOTHING AND NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP CARRIES A CONVERSATION SHE WITNESSED AND A THING SHE PUT A CUP ON.** She was behind that counter on a Wednesday in January while a man told her he had never had a shop bell done, and a man at the far end of it heard the same sentence, and she has not asked either of them what they took from it. She was behind the counter in February when two men argued about a ceiling in a flat above her shop and she put two lots through while they were at it. She was in the room at the back at half past four on the twenty-fourth of February when a box went down on that table, and she put her cup on it. **NOTHING THAT HAPPENED TO HER IN THIS BATCH EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES CARRIES FOUR THINGS HE PUT DOWN AND ONE HE DID NOT.** A churn he has gone round every day since November. A chair he sat in and did not notice. A tin he handed over and could not say why, and took back off a ledge on the Wednesday and put back where it came from. A box on a table he did not look at once. And five words he has not said out loud to anybody. **HE SAID NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER WAS SAID IN FRONT OF HIM IN ANY OF THEM.**

- **THE MAN WHO KEEPS A YARD OFF A SIDE TURNING SWEEPS THE FAR END OF IT AND HAS WATCHED A STRANGER WALK OUT WITH A CRATE HE DID NOT ASK ABOUT.** He is new to this account. He has not thought about that morning and has not told his employer and does not know there is a bench. He came back on a lorry on the third of March and opened a gate for a man who had been waiting some time, and neither of the two of them said anything about the hour.

- **AND IN AN ALCOVE OFF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR IN A BLOCK BY THE WATER, BEHIND A DOOR THAT HAS NEVER CAUGHT, A BOOK STANDS OPEN ON A SHELF.** Nobody has added a twentieth line to it. Whoever wrote the nineteenth wrote it, wrote what he had been told about a lamp outside a yard, and shut the covers to. **THE SPACE UNDER THAT LAST LINE HAD BEEN OPEN ON THE THIRD OF MARCH ACROSS ONE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED AND NINETY-THREE DAYS AND NOBODY PUT A FIGURE ON IT AND NOBODY WENT IN AND NOBODY COUNTED IT.**

- **AND FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ARE STILL GOING ROUND, AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** Which of them a person at that counter is holding has not been said by anybody in the week of the third of March. Nobody has taken one of them back, nobody has renamed the last of them as a fifth, and nothing new has come out of anybody.

- **AND TWO THINGS ON THAT PARADE ARE WRONG AND ARE STILL WRONG.** A shutter at the top of it that has been stopping about a foot above the pavement since the twelfth of February, which the man who puts shutters up lifts twice every morning. A paving slab in the middle of it that has lifted an inch and a half across the whole footway. Both reported to nobody. The woman who works in a shop has walked over the lip of that slab about six hundred times this year and has not mentioned it at the counter to a single person.

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 404 — Wednesday the eighteenth of April 2018, from about half past seven in the morning until about six in the evening, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A DOOR ON A COMMON STAIR PACKED TWICE BY TWO MEN INSIDE A FORTNIGHT, A STRIP OF TORN PAPER STILL IN ITS BOTTOM HINGE, ABOUT THIRTY PENCIL CROSSES ON A WALL IN A CELLAR AND NINE MORE SINCE, A NINE-FOOT LINE OF CHAMFERED BRICK, FOUR BRASS STAIR RODS GIVEN UP IN TEN SECONDS, NINE FOOT OF GARDEN WALL REPOINTED AND RAKED OUT, A THUMBNAIL OF BRICK THAT CANNOT BE PUT BACK, FOUR CHISELS GROUND ON A WHEEL THAT WAS NOT SOMEBODY'S, TWO BANDS OF SLATES UNDER A SHEET OF IRON, A BUNDLE OF LATHS TWO INCHES FORWARD WITH ITS BAND TURNED THE WRONG WAY, A COMB IN A RING OF DUST, A COIL OF CABLE OFF A NAIL, A PIECE OF CORD IN A HALF HITCH, AND FIFTY-FIVE MINUTES OF WIND THAT BROUGHT AN AWNING DOWN. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES IS IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS AND HAS TOLD NOBODY ANYTHING.** A door he packed in half an hour in February and never went back to. A cellar wall he took a batten off and found marks behind, and a man he has not asked about them. A wall he built behind a shop on the same morning another man built a door at the front, and a nine-foot line of arrises he has never walked past. Four rods out of a bag in ten seconds. Nine foot of pointing that got raked out. Four of his own chisels in a garage he was not asked to be in. A comb he put back and a cable he did not. **HE HAS SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND HE HAS BEEN TOLD ALMOST NOTHING, AND NONE OF IT IS A THING ANYBODY PUT A NAME ON.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES IS IN NINE CHAPTERS AND IS NOT IN 0399, WHICH IS CORRECT.** He found somebody's paper in a hinge and worked out whose it was in under a minute and did his own repair and said nothing. He found marks on a cellar wall, counted the new ones twice, and still cannot say what they are. He stood twenty feet off nine foot of new brickwork and knew in two seconds. He was at a door with a bag on while four rods changed hands. He ground four chisels on a wheel that was not his and never found out that anybody was stood at the mouth of that garage. He carried two bands of slates half a mile and left them under iron because the bar was up and the yard was empty. He turned a band on a bundle of laths the right way and left it forward. He has a comb on a sill forty foot from him and does not know. **HE IS ON NOTHING, NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES IS IN EIGHT CHAPTERS.** He said one thing in fifteen seconds about a door and never said it again. He goes down into a cellar most days and is the probable maker of about thirty pencil crosses and has never been asked. He told a man in his thirties that a wall is coming down in June and then watched that man leave it alone. He owns a lock-up he was away from, came back, and found a wiped bench. He put thirty foot of cable on a nail in June of the year before last and has never been in that room. He has a key to a store neither of two men knows the inside of. He was on a low wall for about an hour and a bit with a cup. **HE HAS SAID NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NOTHING ABOUT THE REGISTER WAS SAID IN FRONT OF HIM IN ANY OF THEM.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP IS IN EIGHT CHAPTERS, ALL UNDER HER OWN TAG.** She gave a man she had known a few weeks a key to a cellar in March and has not asked for it back. She heard a lump hammer through a wall for three hours and went out there not once. She was handed four stair rods she had seen on a ledge three weeks earlier and wrote nothing down. She has a shop whose rear wall was rebuilt in a morning. She is the reason two men are both wrong about a bundle of laths, having moved it herself twice to get at a box of hinges behind it and not known that either of them was looking for her. She watched a man sharpen chisels for about a minute and drove off. She heard an awning come down, stood in a doorway, stood at the foot of a pair of steps, and said nothing. **NOTHING THAT HAPPENED TO HER IN THIS BATCH EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF IT CONTAINS A THANKS OF ANY KIND.**

- **THE MAN WHO PUTS SHUTTERS UP TIED AN AWNING WITH A PIECE OF CORD OUT OF HIS POCKET AND WENT ON UP THE PARADE, AND THE MAN WHO DOES THAT HALL PUT HIS HEAD IN A SHUT DOOR AT HALF PAST FOUR AND CAME OUT AGAIN.** The man who works nights went past a gate at half past six on the first of April without stopping and said nothing then and nothing since.

- **AND IN AN ALCOVE OFF A FIRST-FLOOR CORRIDOR IN A BLOCK BY THE WATER, BEHIND A DOOR THAT HAS NEVER CAUGHT, A BOOK STANDS OPEN ON A SHELF.** Nobody has added a twentieth line to it. Whoever wrote the nineteenth wrote it, wrote what he had been told about a lamp outside a yard, and shut the covers to. **THE SPACE UNDER THAT LAST LINE HAD BEEN OPEN ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF APRIL ACROSS ONE THOUSAND TWO HUNDRED AND THIRTY-NINE DAYS AND NOBODY PUT A FIGURE ON IT AND NOBODY WENT IN AND NOBODY COUNTED IT.**

- **AND FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ARE STILL GOING ROUND, AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** Which of them a person at that counter is holding has not been said by anybody in the week of the eighteenth of April. Nobody has taken one of them back, nobody has renamed the last of them as a fifth, and nothing new has come out of anybody.

- **AND TWO THINGS ON THAT PARADE ARE WRONG AND ARE STILL WRONG.** A shutter at the top of it that has been standing about a foot above the pavement since the twelfth of February, which the man who puts shutters up lifts twice every morning. A paving slab in the middle of it that has lifted an inch and a half across the whole footway. Both reported to nobody, and on the eighteenth of April the woman who works in a shop stepped over the lip of the second one going out and put a hand on a door frame she did not need to put a hand on.

- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE FORTY-SIX DAYS WERE FORTY-SIX DAYS AND NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** Shutters in an unaltered order with the last one at the top left to the end of the morning · a counter from ten · a man off nights up that parade at about seven and into it again at about half past four · a man in his sixties on a low wall for about an hour and a bit with a cup · a hall on a meter shut, with the key in and the light on a clock that switched it off at four · about nine people on that parade and not one of them carrying anything for any of the other eight · and one thing going wrong in half an hour of wind that got put right in about nine minutes and told to nobody

- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A piece of paper has been in a slot one thousand one hundred and fifty-three days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post one thousand one hundred and fifty-five days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 395–404 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The counter in the vestibule is in no scene in this batch, answered nothing and was not entered, and **nothing under it is opened, not counted, not asked about and not mentioned, and the word *drawer* is at zero in all ten chapters, as is the word *envelope*.** The rota is not rebuilt and the 11:00–13:30 row is not mentioned. The buff file of 14 October 2011 is not opened and four people have seen the bay number and the date on that cover. Paragraph 4.11 is not answered and the compliance week is not verified. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines included, the word *forty* is at zero, the word *spring* is at zero, the word *winter* is at zero, the word *shed* is at zero, the word *stone* is at zero, the word *card* is at zero, the word *panel* is at zero, the stem *drain* is at zero, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven.** The order is not in force and was not brought back to life.

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 414 — Monday the twenty-eighth of May 2018, from about a quarter past eight in the morning until about half past one in the afternoon, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A CEMENT SHOVEL ON A STACK OF PLASTERBOARD IN AN EMPTY ROOM, A WATER TANK UNDER A STORE WITH A FLOAT VALVE ON IT THAT A MAN REPLACED IN MAY, FOUR WASHING-LINE POSTS IN A YARD, A HARD DARK PLACE IN THE BOTTOM OF A TOOLBAG, A THIRTEEN-INCH DOVETAIL LEVEL IN A VAN THAT WAS ON A SHELF, A RUN OF HEATING PIPE PUT BACK IN AFTER AN HOUR AND A QUARTER OF UNDOING IT, A WIRE BIRDCAGE UNDER A GATE HOOD, FOUR FOOT OF MARBLE ON A SLATE BASE, A DOOR ON A HOT CUPBOARD STANDING TWO INCH OUT FROM ITS FRAME, A DEAL TABLE THAT STOPPED ROCKING, AND TEN MINUTES OF A MAN SITTING ON A STEP IN THE DARK. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES IS IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS AND HAS TOLD NOBODY ANYTHING.** A shovel on a stack, because he had both hands full. A tank he cleaned out because a man who sells pipe said fifteen seconds' worth of nothing. A screwdriver borrowed and a bag opened and a place in the bottom of it looked at for four seconds and not touched. A level off a shelf in a man's own back room. Six minutes of talking at a column. A birdcage under a gate hood. Four foot of marble walked past about thirty times. A step in the dark and six minutes and a sentence said out loud to himself. A length of batten under the short leg of a table. **HE HAS SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND HE HAS BEEN TOLD ALMOST NOTHING, AND NONE OF IT IS A THING ANYBODY PUT A NAME ON.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES IS IN NINE CHAPTERS AND HAS TOLD ONE WOMAN ONE THING SHE ALREADY KNEW, AND 0408 GIVES HIM NO AGE AT ALL.** A shovel stood back against a wall. A minute at a gate. Four post holes and one of them twice. A key handed over at a quarter past eight. A bag of cement and a look at a rack. Four words back at a counter. Twenty foot of pipe and then an hour and a quarter taking it out again. One question about a birdcage door. A stair at about ten to two and a man on the bottom step. **HE IS ON NOTHING, NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES IS IN TWO CHAPTERS, WHICH ARE 0408 AND 0409, AND ASKED NOTHING IN EITHER OF THEM.** He opened a bag he had not opened since about 2014 and decided in four seconds that a mark in the bottom of it was a stove. He stood in his own doorway twice and looked at a clean rectangle thirteen inch wide and went downstairs. **HE IS NOT IN 0405, 0406, 0407, 0410, 0411, 0412, 0413 OR 0414. AN EARLIER TALLY IN THIS FILE SAID FIVE CHAPTERS AND PUT HIM IN 0406, 0407 AND 0412, AND ALL THREE WERE WRONG: THE CUSTOMER IN 0406 IS THE MAN AT THE END OF THAT TERRACE AND IS UNAGED, THE HOUSEHOLDER IN 0407 IS SEVENTY-ONE WITH A BAD HIP AND IS NOT HIM, AND 0412'S CARD ASKED FOR NO SUCH FIGURE. HE HAS NEVER ASKED THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES ABOUT THE LEVEL AND IS NOT GOING TO, AND HE WAS NEVER ASKED ABOUT A TANK OR A WASHING LINE, BECAUSE HE WAS NEVER IN EITHER CHAPTER.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP IS IN SIX CHAPTERS, ALL UNDER HER OWN TAG.** She was ten foot off a man across a counter in a builder's merchant. She was handed a four-second remark about a man with four posts and put it to that man on the Friday and he gave her nothing back. She was behind a square of wired glass for two mornings and watched a whole job. She had no more bags and put a birdcage into a man's hands. She saw a hand stay flat on her counter for two seconds. She found a batten under a table leg and did not go and get him. **NOTHING THAT HAPPENED TO HER IN THIS BATCH EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF IT CONTAINS A THANKS OF ANY KIND.**

THE SAME PASS WENT ON TO THE BOUND AND GOT IT WRONG, AND IT WAS WRONG IN THREE SEPARATE WAYS INSIDE ONE PARAGRAPH: IT TOOK THE TEN-GAP SPAN OF TWENTY-EIGHT TO SIXTY-FOUR DAYS AND COUNTED IT FROM THE FLOOR INSTEAD OF FROM 0414's OWN DATE, WHICH PUT BOTH ENDS A DAY LATE, AT TUESDAY THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JUNE AND WEDNESDAY THE FIRST OF AUGUST 2018; IT PRINTED THE FROM-0414 COUNT AS TWENTY-NINE DAYS AND SIXTY-FIVE DAYS, WHICH IS WRONG AT BOTH ENDS; AND IT ACCUSED THE EARLIER AND CORRECT BOUND OF BEING A DAY EARLY, WHICH IT WAS NOT. A REVIEW OF THAT REPAIR FOUND ALL THREE AND THE LATE PAIR IS OUT OF THE FILES AGAIN. THE FIGURE THAT BINDS IS **MONDAY THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF JUNE 2018 AND TUESDAY THE THIRTY-FIRST OF JULY 2018**, WHICH IS TWENTY-EIGHT AND SIXTY-FOUR DAYS FROM 0414's OWN DATE AND TWENTY-SEVEN AND SIXTY-THREE DAYS FROM THE FLOOR, AND THAT IS THE SAME PAIR BOTH WAYS BECAUSE THE INCOMING GAP IS EXACTLY ONE DAY. NO CHAPTER, NO DATE, NO CARD, NO MAJOR TURN AND NO STANDING WAS TOUCHED TO CORRECT ANY OF IT, AND NOT ONE OF THE TEN DATES IN THIS BATCH IS AFFECTED BY ANY OF IT, BECAUSE EVERY ONE OF THEM WAS WRITTEN AGAINST THE REAL 2018 CALENDAR AND NONE OF THEM WAS MOVED TO MEET A BOUND.**** AND THE SIXTH, WHICH IS ABOUT A CARD: **CARD 0405 ASKS FOR A DISTANCE IN A CLOSED FIGURE AND THE RULE WON AND THE CHAPTER SAYS TEN FOOT, AND THIS IS THE SECOND TIME IN TWO BATCHES THAT A CARD HAS PRINTED A CLOSED FIGURE WHERE IT LOOKS LIKE AN ORDINARY MEASUREMENT.** AND THE SEVENTH, WHICH IS ABOUT A DETECTOR: **THE 0021 RULE FOR A BY-NAME DESCRIPTION EXCLUDES ANY SPAN THAT CONTAINS A DETERMINER AND A FIGURE HEAD NOUN WITH A COMMA NEAR EITHER END, AND IT TOOK THE 326-TOKEN CONTROL DOWN TO 199 ON THE MORNING OF 0022, AND A SPAN IS A DESCRIPTION ONLY IF THE WHOLE SPAN SITS INSIDE THAT ONE NOUN PHRASE.**

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 424 — the Friday of the sixth of July 2018, a whole day, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A LIMB ACROSS A FENCE IN A STREET OFF THAT PARADE WITH A LENGTH OF CLOSE-BOARD OFF ITS NAILS, A PAIR OF READING GLASSES IN A BAG ON A HOOK BEHIND THE SEAT OF A VAN, A STACK OF BOARDS GONE DARK UNDER A ROOF THAT IS NOW SOUND AT ONE END AND STILL WETTING A WALL AT THE OTHER, A NEW PANE IN A STAIR WINDOW WITH PAINTED GLASS BEHIND IT THAT DOES NOT OPEN, A SASH WINDOW STANDING ON ITS EDGE ON GRASS AT THE BOTTOM OF A LANE, TWO NEW HOOKS AND A NEW KEEPER ON A GATE, A STRIP OF STIFF PAPER UNDER A SHOP DOOR THAT CATCHES PEOPLE, A TROWEL ON THE LEDGE OF A BAY AND A BAG OF PLASTER WITH ITS TOP FOLDED OVER IN A CORNER, AND A BICYCLE FOUR FOOT FURTHER IN AGAINST A WALL THAN IT WAS. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES IS IN EIGHT OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND HAS TOLD NOBODY ANYTHING.** A limb he walked past and did not have cut. Four seconds at a counter about a pair of glasses he has got. A ladder and three quarters of an hour on somebody else's roof. Four steps under a window he did not do. A hundred and twenty yard with a window on his shoulder. A true thing about another man, taken in, put down. Three quarters of an hour on a gate he will not explain. A shoulder, a door, and a box of screws at five. A ladder over his shoulder, a doorway, and a man working, and out again. **EVERY JOB HE DID IN THIS BATCH WAS DONE FOR SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT THERE AND NOT ONE OF THEM WAS ASKED FOR, AND HE HAS SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND HAS BEEN TOLD ALMOST NOTHING.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES IS IN NINE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND HAS SAID "NO" TWICE AND NOTHING ELSE ANYBODY HEARD.** A tree he will not have cut. A man at a counter who asked to borrow his glasses and was refused in four seconds. A cistern he went up four flights for and did not do. An offer of a hand with a window that was not taken. A true thing about another man he did nothing with. A gate that was all right, reported to a man who was not in the house. A door coming back off a hinge twice, on two people, in one afternoon, and no word said to either of them. A wall of twelve foot, taken off with a knife and done again in three evenings, with his own bucket carried out every night. **HE IS ON NOTHING, NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES IS IN TWO OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND ASKED NOTHING IN EITHER.** A gate that stopped dragging, and a wall that stopped being half done, and neither of them was found out to be anybody's. **HE IS NOT IN ANY OTHER CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH, AND AN EARLIER TALLY IN THIS FILE PUT HIM IN FIVE, AND THAT TALLY WAS WRONG AND WAS STRUCK IN THE 0022 BLOCK AND HAS NOT BEEN REPEATED.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP IS IN SEVEN OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, ALL UNDER HER OWN TAG.** She asked one question about a tree and got one word. She was on the counter when a man was refused a pair of glasses and she heard both halves, and in the afternoon she asked him for a pencil and got it. She came through a gate with a tube of paper and found a roof sound at one end and a wall wet at the other and went back to her counter. She stood on a step in a block of flats with a bolt in a bag, and then went back three times and put a pane of glass in for nine pounds. She told a man a true thing about another man and did not know she was telling him anything. She stood against the front of her own shop for two minutes with a hand over her arm. She was in a room with a bench in it at twenty past six in the morning with her coat on. **NOTHING THAT HAPPENED TO HER IN THIS BATCH EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF IT CONTAINS A THANKS OF ANY KIND.**

- **AND ONE PERSON IN A SHOP IN THIS BATCH FOUND SOMETHING WRONG, DECIDED WHETHER IT WAS HER BUSINESS, DECIDED IT WAS NOT, AND DID IT ANYWAY THREE WEEKS LATER OUT OF HER OWN PURSE AND AT HER OWN COST, AND TOLD NOBODY, AND WAS NEVER ASKED.** **She is the only person in this batch who began and finished the same piece of work, and neither of the two men who left it alone ever found out that she was in that building.**

- **AND IN A ROOM IN A BLOCK BY THE WATER, BEHIND A DOOR THAT HAS NEVER CAUGHT, A BOOK STANDS OPEN ON A SHELF.** Nobody has added a twentieth line to it. Whoever wrote the nineteenth wrote it, wrote what he had been told about a lamp outside a yard, and shut the covers to. **THE SPACE UNDER THAT LAST LINE had been open across one thousand three hundred and eighteen days WHEN THE SIXTH OF JULY CAME, AND NOBODY PUT A FIGURE ON IT AND NOBODY WENT IN AND NOBODY COUNTED IT.**

- **AND FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ARE STILL GOING ROUND, AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** One of the four is in more mouths than the other three. There is no fifth, none of the four has been taken back, and in three months nobody in this borough has said out loud what they are answers to. It is still a question, and it is a question nobody here is putting to anybody.

- **AND THREE THINGS ON THAT PARADE ARE STILL WRONG AND STILL WRONG.** A shutter at the top of it that has not shut fully since the twelfth of February and that a man lifts twice every morning. A paving slab in the middle of it standing an inch and a half proud across its whole width. The front door of a shop about sixty yard back, which has had nothing holding it back under it since about March and which has caught at least two people in this account. All three reported to nobody, and the third of them is new to this batch and the first two are not, and none of the three was raised in a fortnight by anybody.

- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE THIRTY-NINE DAYS WERE THIRTY-NINE DAYS AND NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** Shutters going down at twenty past six and going back up at half past seven, with a bicycle moved four foot and nobody told · a van outside the shop that shuts early for a while, which then drove away · a man off nights up it in the early afternoon, away up the road · a hall on a meter shut all day with the key in it and a clock putting that building's lights out at four with nobody in it · about nine people out and none of them doing anything for any of the others · and a room at the back of a shop with a bench in it and no table, where a woman ate off a plate in about ten minutes and put the light off.

- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A piece of paper has been in a slot one thousand two hundred and thirty-two days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post one thousand two hundred and thirty-four days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 415–424 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The second line under Bram Osei's Thursday has not been looked at. The board is at seven of nine. The ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and untold. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines included, the word *forty* is at zero, the word *spring* is at zero, the word *winter* is at zero, the word *shed* is at zero, the word *stone* is at zero, the word *card* is at zero, the word *panel* is at zero, the word *tin* is at zero, the word *bracket* is at zero, the word *rail* is at zero, the stem *drain* is at zero, the stem *requir* is at zero, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven and nothing in this batch is a twelfth.**

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 434 — the Tuesday of the twenty-first of August 2018, from about four in the afternoon until about half past nine that night, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: A CAST IRON STACK THAT WAS OFF A ROOF IN 2017 AND WAS OFFERED ONCE, A SAUCER WITH A CHIP IN IT IN A DOOR POCKET, A SCREWDRIVER ON SOME FLAGS UNDER A BLOCK OF THREE, A PICTURE FRAME STANDING ON A SHELF IN A STORE ROOM AND A CAST IRON RANGE STILL IN THE SAME BUILDING, A REEL OF FLEX ON A HOOK IN A YARD, EIGHT MONTHS OF SMOKE IN ONE FRONT ROOM, A MAT ON A STEP WITH A TYRE PRINT ON THE BACK OF IT, AND A RULED SPACE UNDER A NINETEENTH LINE. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES DID SEVEN THINGS IN FORTY-SIX DAYS AND TOLD NOBODY ABOUT ONE OF THEM.** He stood at an open van and heard something true about another man and did nothing. He put his hand on some iron for ten minutes and left it. He lay across three boards and left them. He said at a counter that he had never been inside a building he had been inside. He had his hand filled with a saucer. He carried thirty foot of flex out of a yard and put it back on the same hook. He carried a picture frame out of a room he had no business in and left it in another room of the same building. **HE WAS TOLD TWO THINGS IN ALL THOSE DAYS AND DID NEITHER OF THEM WITH ANYTHING, AND HE WENT HOME EVERY NIGHT AT THE SAME TIME.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES DID EIGHT THINGS AND SAID "NO" ONCE.** He answered a question about his eating and carried it for six weeks. He took a kitchen unit out of a flat with a man on the roof above him. He said he had never been in a building. He let a saucer be put in his hand. He watched a man take a reel and shut his mouth about it. He wanted a sink and did not get it. He spent ninety minutes in a house looking at a chimney and then took his money. He opened a front for a man and stepped back and picked his tape measure up. **HE IS ON NOTHING, NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES APPEARED FOUR TIMES, SAID ONE SENTENCE THAT WAS NOT A QUESTION, AND DECIDED ONE THING.** He told a man at a van that another man has no telephone. He watched a screwdriver land and did not go out. He was told no about a yard and believed it. He found a bare hook and watched a reel go back up and decided, in his own coat, that thirty foot of flex is not an evening of his life. **HE HAS BEEN ASKED NOTHING IN ANY OF THE FOUR AND HE WILL NOT BE ASKED, AND HE STILL DOES NOT KNOW THAT THE MAN IN THE THIRTIES WAS UNDER THE MAN IN THE FORTIES.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP IS IN FIVE OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND SOMETHING HAPPENED TO HER IN ALL FIVE, AND THE FIRST TELLING OF THIS LINE SAID TEN AND LISTED EIGHT ACTS AND BOTH HALVES OF THAT WERE WRONG.** At a counter on the seventh of July she put to a man in his thirties a question about how he was eating, which was not a question, and did not know she had said anything. On the sixteenth she asked him about a building at the top of that street and believed a sentence about it that was not true, and was told it four more times. On the twentieth she put a saucer into the free hand of that same man and went back round her counter without a word. In August she opened two rooms over her own shop, left them unlocked, and asked for a figure on a range and not a valuation. On the twenty-first of August she walked out of the front of her own shop with a customer standing at the counter, carried a mat back onto its step with it against her hip, wiped the grit off it with her hand, and went back in and finished with the customer she had left standing. **SHE IS ALSO NAMED ONCE IN THE CLOSING BLOCK OF 0426 AS A PERSON SHE DID NOT TELL, AND IS BEHIND A COUNTER IN 0431 FOR A MINUTE AND A HALF WHILE A MAN BUYS SCREWS HE DOES NOT NEED, AND IS NOT IN 0427, 0430, 0432 OR 0434 UNDER ANY OTHER NAME. THREE OF THE EIGHT ACTS THE FIRST TELLING ATTRIBUTED TO HER WERE NOT HERS: THE SINK CAME TO THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES FROM A MAN WHO DEALS IN PIPE AND NOT FROM HER, THE HOUSE WITH THE BLACK STREAK IS A DIFFERENT WOMAN WHO LIVES AT THE END OF A SHORT ROW AND DOES NOT WORK IN A SHOP, AND THE FOUR ANSWERS WERE DISCUSSED BY TWO MEN OUTSIDE A SHUTTER WHILE SHE WAS INSIDE A LOCK-UP.** NOTHING THAT HAPPENED TO HER IN THIS BATCH EXISTS ANYWHERE OUTSIDE IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF IT CONTAINS A THANKS OF ANY KIND.

- **AND ONE WOMAN IN THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN LIVING WITH A THING SINCE DECEMBER AND TOLD NOBODY, AND ONE MAN LOOKED AT IT IN AUGUST AND SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY ABOUT IT.** She swept a flue in November and it was clear. She dried washing in a room that was filling with smoke and moved a settee six inch out of it. She has a cough. Her son rings on Sundays. **He asked her two questions in a room she has been sitting in since the winter, got answers, went back to his own work in that room for fifty minutes more, took his money and left, AND BY HALF PAST SEVEN THAT EVENING SHE HAD LIT THAT FIRE AGAIN AND WILL GO ON LIGHTING IT.**

- **AND A LOCK-UP AT THE TOP OF THAT PARADE HAD ITS SHUTTER UP FROM TEN UNTIL NINE WITH A MAN IN IT REPAIRING THINGS THAT ARE NOT WORTH REPAIRING, AND ABOUT THIRTY PEOPLE WENT PAST THAT OPENING AND NOT ONE OF THEM LOOKED IN.** Nobody on that parade has spoken to him in about seven months. **THE ONE PLACE ON THAT PARADE THAT NINE PEOPLE WERE ON THAT EVENING IS A PLACE THEY WERE ON SEPARATELY, AND NONE OF THOSE NINE WAS THERE ON BEHALF OF ANY OF THE OTHER EIGHT.**

- **AND IN A ROOM IN A BLOCK BY THAT WATER, BEHIND A PLACE THAT HAS NEVER CAUGHT, A BOOK STANDS OPEN ON A SHELF.** Nobody has added a twentieth line to it. Whoever wrote the nineteenth wrote it, wrote what he had been told about a lamp outside a yard, and shut the covers to. **THE SPACE UNDER THAT LAST LINE had been open across one thousand three hundred and sixty-four days WHEN THE TWENTY-FIRST OF AUGUST CAME, AND NOBODY PUT A FIGURE ON IT AND NOBODY WENT IN AND NOBODY COUNTED IT.**

- **AND FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ARE STILL GOING ROUND, AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** One of the four is in more mouths than the other three. There is no fifth, none of the four has been taken back, and in August a man told another man on a pavement that he knew what something was, and the other man said he did not, and nobody said what any of them is an answer to, and nobody was asked one that day. It is still a question, and it is a question nobody here is putting to anybody else.

- **AND THERE IS STILL SOMETHING IN THIS BOROUGH THAT NOT ONE PERSON LIVING IN IT COULD DESCRIBE IF YOU WERE TO STOP ANY OF THEM AND ASK, AND IT IS THE SAME SOMETHING IT WAS A MONTH AGO, AND IT HAS NOT MOVED, AND NOBODY HAS MENTIONED IT.** Nobody is going to be asked about it and nobody is going to answer it and nobody in this borough knows what it is, which is a condition and not a mystery.

## THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 444 — the Wednesday of the third of October 2018, from about seven in the morning until about midday, in this city

**WHAT THIS BOROUGH IS CARRYING AT THE END OF IT: TWO FOLDED BLANKETS DONE UP IN STRING, ONE IN THE BACK OF A VAN AND ONE UNDER A STAIR, BOTH FOR A WOMAN AT BRACKNESS AND NEITHER OF THEM DELIVERED; A CLEAN RECTANGLE IN THE DUST OF A TABLE IN A PASSAGE; A STUMP THAT IS HALF AN INCH PROUDER OF THE ROAD; A HALF-LANDING LIT FOUR MORNINGS A WEEK; SIX LENGTHS OF FENCE BACK ON SIX POSTS WITH NO NAME ON ANY OF IT; SIX ROLLS OUT OF A CHANNEL AND BACK ON A TRAY; A SASH WINDOW THAT OPENS FOUR INCH; AND A RULED SPACE UNDER A NINETEENTH LINE THAT WAS OPEN FOR ONE THOUSAND FOUR HUNDRED AND SEVEN DAYS WHEN THE MORNING CAME. NOTHING WAS RESOLVED AND NOBODY WAS ASKED ABOUT ANY OF IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES DID SEVEN THINGS IN THE FORTY-THREE DAYS FROM THE TWENTY-FIRST OF AUGUST TO THE THIRD OF OCTOBER, AND AN EIGHTH THE DAY BEFORE THE FIRST OF THEM, AND TOLD NOBODY ABOUT FOUR.** He carried a blanket he was given in June and did not take it to the woman it was for, because he does not know which house she is in. He was on a corner of tarmac with a crowbar and a man he does not know, and neither of them said why, and they have not said a word to each other since. He stood on a landing in a block he lives in that had been dark since June, and went up, and came back down, and thought about nothing. He said a true thing at a shop on a Monday and went out to his van. He walked a path in the afternoon, read a line of fence that had not been there on the Wednesday, worked out inside half a minute which man had put it up, and said nothing to anybody. He looked at a stool at eight in the morning and thought it had been put out and left. He stood at the top of that parade on the twenty-first of August and again in the morning of the third of October, and on the third of October he went into a channel on his hands and knees and picked four rolls out of it. **HE WAS TOLD ONE THING IN ALL THOSE DAYS AND DID NOTHING WITH IT, AND HE WENT HOME EVERY NIGHT AT THE SAME TIME.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES DID SIX THINGS IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND SPOKE TO TWO PEOPLE HE DID NOT KNOW, ONE OF THEM FOR TWENTY MINUTES AND THE OTHER OF THEM FOR ONE SENTENCE.** He got his own bag out from behind a case and read a label. He helped a man he had never spoken to put a foot square of tarmac back crooked over a stump, and each of them went away believing the other was talking about the same thing. He built six lengths of fence in one day out of a ditch for nobody and put nothing on any of it. He ate off a plate on a low wall at ten past two for about a month. He was in a flat for the week from Monday to Sunday and took a bed out from under a window on the Thursday and put it back half an inch out. He walked past a stool at half past ten at night and stood on a turning head for two minutes. **HE IS ON NOTHING, NOBODY HAS GIVEN HIM ANYTHING TO PUT A NAME ON, AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS THAT HE WILL DO A THING AND NOT DONE IT.**

- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES GAVE SOMEBODY A BLANKET IN JUNE AND HAS NOT ASKED HIM FOR IT BACK, AND HE PUT A MAN'S NAME HE WAS GIVEN IN A SHOP TO NOTHING, AND HE STOOD WITH A CUP ON A CORNER EVERY AFTERNOON AT TEN PAST TWO.** He also said *Aye* to a woman on a Saturday morning and turned back to the shop floor. **HE HAS ASKED NOTHING IN ANY OF THESE FOUR AND WILL NOT BE ASKED, AND HE DOES NOT KNOW THAT A MAN WHO SAT ON THAT CORNER LAST MONTH HAD ONCE SEEN A WOMAN GO DOWN ON A LIP IN A ROAD.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP WAS TOLD ONE TRUE THING AND BUILT SIX DAYS OUT OF HALF OF IT, AND THEN TOOK A TRAY OUT OF HER OWN FRONT DOOR AND DROPPED IT.** She moved a delivery to the wrong end of that parade and lost a day out of her week. She stood in the wind at half past eight with a carrier bag on a Friday. She said to a man of her father's age that somebody up on that street was minding things, and saw what that cost him, and has not mentioned any of it to anybody. On the third of October she came out of her own door with two dozen rolls on a tray, put her heel off the edge of the flag, said *That's it, that's it* four times and *Well* once to nobody, and put the tray on the stack at the top and brought it in at ten with all of them the right way up. **SHE HAS TOLD NOBODY AND IS NOT GOING TO.**

- **AND ONE WOMAN PUT FOUR BULBS IN A STAIR IN A BUILDING SHE CLEANS IN AND NOBODY FOUND OUT, AND FIVE PEOPLE STOOD IN AN L OF NINE FOOT AT TEN PAST TWO FOR A MONTH AND NOT ONE OF THEM KNEW ABOUT THE OTHER FOUR.** The woman on the first floor goes out for a cigarette she does not smoke indoors. The man with a cup stands, because going down low and standing up again would mean putting a hand on a wall in full daylight. The woman at the end carries her plate out to that side of her table because that is where the daylight falls on it. The man with a van stands and looks up a gable. **THE ONLY TIME ANY OF THEM CAME NEAR ANOTHER WAS THE ONCE THE MAN WITH THE VAN CAME ROUND THAT CORNER AND FOUND THE WOMAN EIGHT FOOT OFF, AND HE WAITED THERE WHILE SHE FINISHED, AND HE HAS NOT SPOKEN OF IT SINCE.**

- **AND A MAN IN HIS FORTIES AND A MAN IN HIS THIRTIES READ THE SAME STOOL FOURTEEN HOURS APART AND BOTH OF THEM WERE RIGHT, AND A SECOND MAN READ A CASE ON A TABLE AND A THIRD WOMAN READ A BED AND FOUR INCHES, AND NONE OF THE THREE HAS SAID ANY OF IT TO ANYBODY.**

- **AND IN A ROOM IN A BLOCK BY THAT WATER, BEHIND A PLACE THAT HAS NEVER CAUGHT, A BOOK STANDS OPEN ON A SHELF.** Nobody has added a twentieth line to it. **THE SPACE UNDER THAT LAST LINE had been open across one thousand four hundred and seven days WHEN THE THIRD OF OCTOBER CAME, AND NOBODY PUT A FIGURE ON IT AND NOBODY WENT IN AND NOBODY COUNTED IT.**

- **AND FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ARE STILL GOING ROUND, AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** One was said outside a shop at ten past nine on the third of October and was answered with one syllable. There is no fifth, none of the four has been taken back, and nobody has said what any of them is an answer to, and nobody was asked one that day. It is still a question, and it is a question nobody here is putting to anybody else.

- **AND THERE IS STILL SOMETHING IN THIS BOROUGH THAT NOT ONE PERSON LIVING IN IT COULD PUT INTO WORDS IF YOU STOPPED THEM IN THE STREET, AND IT IS THE SAME SOMETHING IT WAS A FORTNIGHT AGO, AND IT HAS NOT MOVED, AND NOBODY HAS MENTIONED IT.**

- **AND THE BOROUGH IS ORDINARY AND THE FORTY-THREE DAYS WERE FORTY-THREE DAYS AND NOT A TURN OF ANYTHING.** Two people carried a blanket each for the same woman and did not meet · a case went out of a passage on a train or did not · a stump moved half an inch and the tarmac went back crooked · four bulbs and a landing · six lengths of fence in a day with nothing written on them · five people in the same nine foot of wall · a sentence taken half of and built into six days · a bed put back half an inch out · a stool read twice and gone by Saturday · and a tray of rolls that went into a channel and came back up.

- **AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS WHERE IT WAS.** A piece of paper has been in a slot one thousand three hundred and twenty-one days and has not been taken out, put up or read, and it is in no chapter of this batch. A letter has been in a tray on the second floor under two other pieces of post one thousand three hundred and twenty-three days, sealed, and **no chapter of chapters 435–444 went near that room.** The page of 29 May 2015 is not in force and its one reader has not been asked again. The nine printed sheets of 19 June still read *no requirement stated* and have no name box. The column headed *date seen again* is still empty. The counter in the vestibule is in no scene in this batch, answered nothing and was not entered, and **nothing under it is opened, not counted, not asked about and not mentioned, and the word *drawer* is at zero in all ten chapters, as is the word *envelope*.** The rota is not rebuilt and the 11:00–13:30 row is not mentioned. The second line under Bram Osei's Thursday has not been looked at. The board is at seven of nine. The ninth seat is fifty-four and unpaid and untold. The buff file of 14 October 2011 is not opened and four people have seen the bay number and the date on that cover. Paragraph 4.11 is not answered and the compliance week is not verified. Roy Albery does not walk again. **Nobody died, nobody was rescued, nobody was housed by anybody, nobody renewed, expired or renamed anything, nothing was minuted, no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind, the word *thank* is at zero in all ten, the word *eleven* is at zero in all ten with the datelines included, the word *forty* is at zero, the word *spring* is at zero, the word *winter* is at zero, the word *autumn* is at zero, the word *shed* is at zero, the word *stone* is at zero, the word *card* is at zero, the word *panel* is at zero, the word *tin* is at zero, the word *bracket* is at zero, the word *rail* is at zero, the word *chair* is at zero, the word *lamp* is at zero, the stem *drain* is at zero, the stem *requir* is at zero, and the eleven standing refusals are eleven.**

- **AND THE SIX OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE WAS ANSWERED. From 0184, which dated thing will be the first somebody can prove — **still open, and it went the same way for the fourteenth batch in a row, and the four answers cannot be put beside one another, and a fifth has not joined them and is not going to. From 0174, which of the eight will be the first somebody decides to keep — **untouched. From 0164, whether the page or the chair is the first thing a person is asked to agree to — **untouched, and a case left a passage, a stump got an inch proud, a landing got four bulbs, a fence came back up, a window opened four inch, a stool went away, a tray went down and came back up, and a sentence was turned into six days by somebody who took half of it, and not one of the eight was asked about. From 0192, which of two opposite shapes this city has to stop, and what a person has to be holding to stop each — **still open, and the second of them is still nothing. The volume's own, about a permission, an obligation and a right of return — **untouched, and the word *permission* is at zero in all ten chapters. And the sixth, about a document and not a person — **no piece of paper that leaves a building was created in this batch, and the things that were put down, given, taken, used, put back, lit or put right were two blankets, a case, a crowbar, four bulbs, six lengths of fence, six rolls and a tray, and nobody has a copy of any of it, and a label tied through a handle is a piece of paper that went nowhere.**

- **AND SEVEN FLAGS GO TO A HUMAN AND NONE OF THEM IS A WRITER'S.
